| shifted | .compass/research/human-surface/RESEARCH-screenplays-in-the-vault.md | 226 | a714ee4 | plugin/cli/vaultlib.py:69-96 | def classify_root_dirs(root):<br>    """Classify every subdirectory of `root` (the vault root, or a unit folder<br>    when scanning the unit's own type dirs).<br><br>    Returns `{"type_dirs": [...], "units": [...], "unclassified": [...]}`,<br>    each a name list in sorted order. Reserved infra dirs (NON_TYPE_DIRS and<br>    dot-dirs) are skipped outright. Reserved type names are always type dirs.<br>    A non-reserved dir is a unit iff its `index.md` carries the `type: unit`<br>    marker; failing that it is a custom type dir when it holds a typed<br>    artifact (the content signal that keeps e.g. `retro/` working); failing<br>    that, if it contains markdown at any depth it is unclassified - never<br>    scanned, surfaced by `validate` for the human to resolve. Directories<br>    with no markdown at all are silently ignored.<br>    """<br>    root = Path(root)<br>    result = {"type_dirs": [], "units": [], "unclassified": []}<br>    for child in sorted(root.iterdir()):<br>        if not child.is_dir() or child.name in NON_TYPE_DIRS or child.name.startswith("."):<br>            continue<br>        if child.name in CORE_TYPE_DIRS:<br>            result["type_dirs"].append(child.name)<br>        elif _is_unit(child):<br>            result["units"].append(child.name)<br>        elif _has_typed_artifact(child):<br>            result["type_dirs"].append(child.name)<br>        elif any(child.rglob("*.md")):<br>            result["unclassified"].append(child.name)<br>    return result | def classify_root_dirs(root):<br>    """Classify every subdirectory of `root` (the vault root, or a unit folder<br>    when scanning the unit's own type dirs).<br><br>    Returns `{"type_dirs": [...], "units": [...], "unclassified": [...]}`,<br>    each a name list in sorted order. Reserved infra dirs (NON_TYPE_DIRS and<br>    dot-dirs) are skipped outright. Reserved type names are always type dirs.<br>    A non-reserved dir is a unit iff its `index.md` carries the `type: unit`<br>    marker; failing that it is a custom type dir when it holds a typed<br>    artifact (the content signal that keeps e.g. `retro/` working); failing<br>    that, if it contains markdown at any depth it is unclassified - never<br>    scanned, surfaced by `validate` for the human to resolve. Directories<br>    with no markdown at all are silently ignored.<br>    """<br>    root = Path(root)<br>    result = {"type_dirs": [], "units": [], "unclassified": []}<br>    for child in sorted(root.iterdir()):<br>        if not child.is_dir() or child.name in NON_TYPE_DIRS or child.name.startswith("."):<br>            continue<br>        if child.name in CORE_TYPE_DIRS:<br>            result["type_dirs"].append(child.name)<br>        elif _is_unit(child):<br>            result["units"].append(child.name)<br>        elif _has_typed_artifact(child):<br>            result["type_dirs"].append(child.name)<br>        elif any(child.rglob("*.md")):<br>            result["unclassified"].append(child.name)<br>    return result | plugin/cli/vaultlib.py:70-97 |

Sort key, highest priority first: any row with<br>    `escalated` set sorts before every row without it, regardless of the<br>    scores below; then tag overlap; then area match; then text overlap;<br>    then `score` as the final tiebreak; then `file` for a deterministic<br>    order among rows tied on everything else.<br><br>    When the query carries any criteria, a row matching none of them is<br>    excluded rather than kept as low-ranked backfill - surfacing an<br>    unrelated lesson as "relevant" is noise, and the retrieval trace<br>    records what this returns as surfaced. Escalated rows are exempt from<br>    the exclusion. Look for `> **Last session:**` breadcrumbs on plan files.<br><br>4. **Verify current state.**<br>   ```bash<br>   git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD<br>   git rev-parse --short HEAD<br>   git log --oneline <handoff_commit>..HEAD<br>   git status --short<br>   ```<br><br>   Check each critical file: does it still exist? Modified since the handoff?<br><br>5. **Classify scenario:**<br>   - **A. Clean continuation** - same branch, same/ahead commit, no unexpected changes → resume from action items.<br>   - **B. Diverged** - different branch OR commits by others since handoff → flag, show what changed, then ask how to reconcile through `AskUserQuestion`, exactly one question per call: the recommended option first, labelled "(Recommended)", each option one short line saying what choosing it does, "Other" left open for free text. Never batch several questions in one message or one call.<br>   - **C. Incomplete work** - uncommitted changes not in handoff → flag, present, ask for intent.<br>   - **D. Stale** - >7 days old OR >20 commits since handoff → treat handoff as historical context only, recommend fresh summary.<br><br>6. **Present the situational report:**<br><br>   ```markdown<br>   ## Handoff Resume: [handoff title]<br><br>   ### State<br>   - Scenario: [A/B/C/D]<br>   - Handoff age: [N days]<br>   - Branch: [current] (handoff: [branch])<br>   - Commit: [current] (handoff: [hash])<br>   - Commits since handoff: N<br>   - Plan breadcrumb: [if found]<br><br>   ### Divergences<br>   [What changed that the handoff didn't expect]<br><br>   ### File Verification<br>   \| File \| Handoff State \| Current \| Match? \|<br>   \|------\|---------------\|---------\|--------\|<br>   \| `file.py` \| Modified at :42 \| Unchanged \| Yes \|<br><br>   ### Action Items from Handoff<br>   1. [ ] [Item] - [still relevant?]<br><br>   ### Recommended Approach<br>   [Based on scenario]<br><br>   ### Relevant Lessons<br>   [Lessons that apply]<br>   ```<br><br>7. **After human confirms:** mark handoff `status: done`, update `active.md` if statuses need correction, proceed with the first action item. | ## Mode: Resume<br><br>The handoff is a snapshot. | shifted | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-taxonomy-for-unambiguous-placement.md | 418 | 002a0cf | plugin/cli/vaultlib.py:304-326 | def resolvable_names_map(vault_root):<br>    """Every name a wikilink may resolve to, mapped to the vault-relative<br>    POSIX paths (extension kept) of the files bearing that name.<br><br>    Names per file: its bare stem, its path-qualified name (vault-relative,<br>    no extension), and - when the file is a folder `index.md` - the folder's<br>    name and the folder's vault-relative path. Drawn from every markdown<br>    file in the vault, so archive/ and custom type dirs are covered. A name<br>    mapping to more than one path is an ambiguous wikilink.<br>    """<br>    vault_root = Path(vault_root)<br>    mapping = {}<br>    for path in all_markdown_files(vault_root):<br>        rel = path.relative_to(vault_root)<br>        names = {path.stem, str(rel.with_suffix("")).replace("\\", "/")}<br>        if path.name == "index.md":<br>            names.add(path.parent.name)<br>            folder_rel = rel.parent.as_posix()<br>            if folder_rel != ".":<br>                names.add(folder_rel)<br>        for name in names:<br>            mapping.setdefault(name, []).append(str(rel).replace("\\", "/"))<br>    return mapping | def resolvable_names_map(vault_root):<br>    """Every name a wikilink may resolve to, mapped to the vault-relative<br>    POSIX paths (extension kept) of the files bearing that name.<br><br>    Names per file: its bare stem, its path-qualified name (vault-relative,<br>    no extension), and - when the file is a folder `index.md` - the folder's<br>    name and the folder's vault-relative path. Drawn from every markdown<br>    file in the vault, so archive/ and custom type dirs are covered. A name<br>    mapping to more than one path is an ambiguous wikilink.<br>    """<br>    vault_root = Path(vault_root)<br>    mapping = {}<br>    for path in all_markdown_files(vault_root):<br>        rel = path.relative_to(vault_root)<br>        names = {path.stem, str(rel.with_suffix("")).replace("\\", "/")}<br>        if path.name == "index.md":<br>            names.add(path.parent.name)<br>            folder_rel = rel.parent.as_posix()<br>            if folder_rel != ".":<br>                names.add(folder_rel)<br>        for name in names:<br>            mapping.setdefault(name, []).append(str(rel).replace("\\", "/"))<br>    return mapping | plugin/cli/vaultlib.py:460-482 |

| shifted | .compass/research/whole-overhead/RESEARCH-axis-2-attribution.md | 48 | c8b465b | bench/README.md:26-33 | ## Arms<br><br>Both arms call the same Claude Code binary against the same model with the same tool allowlist and the same turn budget. The only difference is what is installed in the workspace before the agent starts.<br><br>- **compass arm**: `agents/compass-agent.sh` scaffolds a fresh `.compass/` vault inside the task workspace and copies the plugin's templates and skills into `.claude/`. Claude Code then runs the task with the full methodology, the agent definitions, the skills, and the hooks.<br>- **baseline arm**: `agents/baseline-agent.sh` runs Claude Code directly against the task workspace with no methodology, no agent definitions, no skills, no hooks.<br><br>Both arms record the model, tool allowlist, turn budget, total tokens, wall-clock time, and the benchmark's own success score. | ## Arms<br><br>Both arms call the same Claude Code binary against the same model with the same tool allowlist and the same turn budget. The only difference is what is installed in the workspace before the agent starts.<br><br>- **compass arm**: `agents/compass-agent.sh` scaffolds a fresh `.compass/` vault inside the task workspace and copies the plugin's templates and skills into `.claude/`. Claude Code then runs the task with the full methodology, the agent definitions, the skills, and the hooks.<br>- **baseline arm**: `agents/baseline-agent.sh` runs Claude Code directly against the task workspace with no methodology, no agent definitions, no skills, no hooks.<br><br>Both arms record the model, tool allowlist, turn budget, total tokens, wall-clock time, and the benchmark's own success score. | bench/README.md:29-36 |

| shifted | .compass/research/whole-overhead/RESEARCH-axis-6-build-success.md | 205 | c8b465b | bench/README.md:59-65 | ## Reporting<br><br>`analyze.py` reports the Pareto front over (success rate, total tokens, total cost). The point of the Pareto framing is that Compass adds tokens (more agent hops, more reads); the value claim must net those costs. Raw `pass@1` alone is not enough.<br><br>## Reading the results<br><br>A run that shows higher success rate at equal or lower cost than baseline is a clean Compass win. A run that shows equal success at higher cost is a loss even if `pass@1` is unchanged. A run that shows higher cost AND lower success is what the field literature warns about - frameworks that consume tokens without delivering quality. | ## Reporting<br><br>`analyze.py` reports the Pareto front over (success rate, total tokens, total cost). The point of the Pareto framing is that Compass adds tokens (more agent hops, more reads); the value claim must net those costs. Raw `pass@1` alone is not enough.<br><br>## Reading the results<br><br>A run that shows higher success rate at equal or lower cost than baseline is a clean Compass win. A run that shows equal success at higher cost is a loss even if `pass@1` is unchanged. A run that shows higher cost AND lower success is what the field literature warns about - frameworks that consume tokens without delivering quality. | bench/README.md:62-68 |

When it fits a domain but no subdomain cleanly, file it directly in the domain - shallower beats a wrong guess. A vault with no domains files at the root; that is correct, not provisional. The folder shape is earned by the second member: when a spec gains its first child, `compass promote` turns it into `SPEC-NNN-name/index.md` and the child is created inside, numbered locally (`compass next-num spec <parent-path>`). Open Questions holds only a question the human himself left open, in his words; the agent never mints one. | changed | .compass/plans/PLAN-027-numbered-choices.md | 52 | 67b7829 | plugin/templates/rules/compass-pipeline.md:16 | Ask through `AskUserQuestion`, exactly one question per call: the recommended option first and labelled "(Recommended)", each option one short line saying what choosing it does, and the free-text "Other" left open. Several questions are never batched in one message or one call; the next question waits for this answer. A subagent cannot call `AskUserQuestion`: it stops and returns the question to the orchestrator, with its options and the recommended one marked, and the orchestrator asks it the same way. | Ask through `AskUserQuestion`, exactly one question per call: the options are a numbered list, the recommended one first and labelled "(Recommended)", each one short line saying what choosing it does, the free-text "Other" left open, and a reply of just a number picks that option. The tool numbers its options, so labels carry none; a choice shown as text, as on a host without the tool, is written as `1.`, `2.`, `3.` lines. Several questions are never batched in one message or one call; the next question waits for this answer. A subagent cannot call `AskUserQuestion`: it stops and returns the question to the orchestrator, its options numbered and the recommended one marked, and the orchestrator asks it the same way. |  |
| changed | .compass/plans/PLAN-027-numbered-choices.md | 53 | 67b7829 | plugin/templates/rules/compass-pipeline.md:18 | **Review in a visual surface.** When a gate's subject is a document and a review surface is configured, the document itself reaches the human, opening with its picture and a "You decide" callout, any guess the agent made marked inline; no walkthrough of it is written in chat. Commit, then run `compass review <file>... --title "<the gate's question>"` in the background; a gate with several documents (a plan and its detail file, a spec and its research) sends them as one review, `--why "<file>=<line>"` saying what to check in each. On `requested <id>`, end the turn with one line naming the document's title, id and commit, and nothing else; `AskUserQuestion` is not called. An approved verdict is the gate's recommended approve option, its notes and `comment` lines steering what follows, and any follow-up question the gate would ask next takes its recommended option unless the notes say otherwise; at approval the "You decide" callout leaves the file, since an approved document states what is. A changes verdict applies the notes, the `comment` lines and the edits (the `edited` lines and `git diff`), then sends the revised document again the same way, opening with a "Since your last review" callout listing what changed. A reply typed in the terminal while the review is pending runs `compass review --cancel <id>`, and that reply is the answer. On `not configured`, the gate asks through `AskUserQuestion` as the paragraph above describes, and the document's content is shown as plain text. A host with no background wake-up runs the command in the foreground. | **Review in a visual surface.** When a gate's subject is a document and a review surface is configured, the document itself reaches the human, opening with its picture and a "You decide" callout, any guess the agent made marked inline; no walkthrough of it is written in chat. Commit, then run `compass review <file>... --title "<the gate's question>"` in the background; a gate with several documents (a plan and its detail file, a spec and its research) sends them as one review, `--why "<file>=<line>"` saying what to check in each. On `requested <id>`, end the turn with one line naming the document's title, id and commit, and nothing else; `AskUserQuestion` is not called. An approved verdict is the gate's recommended approve option, its notes and `comment` lines steering what follows, and any follow-up question the gate would ask next takes its recommended option unless the notes say otherwise; at approval the "You decide" callout leaves the file, since an approved document states what is. A changes verdict applies the notes, the `comment` lines and the edits (the `edited` lines and `git diff`), then sends the revised document again the same way, opening with a "Since your last review" callout listing what changed. When a review lists several documents, the human answers each one on its own and `compass review` ends on the first answer, printing that document's `file` line and one `still open <id>: ...` line naming the rest. That `file` line's verdict applies to that document alone: an approved document is handled as approved, a changes document is revised and sent again on its own. Then run `compass review --wait <id>` in the background for the documents still open, and repeat until no `still open` line follows. A reply typed in the terminal while the review is pending runs `compass review --cancel <id>`, and that reply is the answer. A `waiting:` line from `compass review` changes nothing for the agent: the review stays open until the human answers. On `not configured`, the gate asks through `AskUserQuestion` as the paragraph above describes, and the document's content is shown as plain text. A host with no background wake-up runs the command in the foreground. |  |

| changed | .compass/plans/PLAN-032-consolidation-grounded-in-sources.md | 72 | 9a33f39 | plugin/cli/lessonslib.py:1-21 | """Catalog row reader and ranking for `compass lessons`.<br><br>Reads `.compass/meta/lessons-catalog.yaml` rows only, never lesson bodies.<br>The catalog is machine-generated by `compass sync` (`commands/sync.py`,<br>`_catalog_row`) with a fixed row shape: a `- file: "<name>"` line followed<br>by `status`, `category`, `area`, `tags` (an inline `[a, b]` list), `score`<br>(an integer), and `summary`, each indented four spaces. A row may also<br>carry `escalated`, written by `_catalog_row` for a lesson whose frontmatter<br>has it and stamped onto an existing row by `lesson-write` at escalation<br>time (sync appends rows but never rewrites existing ones).<br>The parser mirrors this shape exactly and fails loud - naming the row<br>number - on a row that does not match it, rather than silently returning a<br>short list.<br><br>Ranking has no graph substrate: lesson rows carry no `depends_on` or<br>wikilink edges (SPEC-012 D-04), so retrieval is tag overlap, area match,<br>and summary-token overlap over the flat row list, with `score` as the<br>final tiebreak. A row carrying `escalated` always sorts first: escalation<br>means retrieval already missed or a reader already rejected the lesson<br>three times, which is the failure this design surfaces rather than hides.<br>""" | """Catalog row reader and ranking for `compass lessons`.<br><br>Reads `.compass/meta/lessons-catalog.yaml` rows only, never lesson bodies.<br>The catalog is machine-generated by `compass sync` (`commands/sync.py`,<br>`_catalog_row`) with a fixed row shape: a `- file: "<name>"` line followed<br>by `status`, `category`, `area`, `tags` (an inline `[a, b]` list), `score`<br>(an integer), and `summary`, each indented four spaces. A row may also<br>carry `escalated`, written by `_catalog_row` for a lesson whose frontmatter<br>has it. Sync owns every row: it appends missing rows and rewrites an<br>existing row whenever the lesson file's frontmatter differs from it, except<br>that `summary` follows the file only while the row's old text survives in<br>the lesson file.<br>The parser mirrors this shape exactly and fails loud - naming the row<br>number - on a row that does not match it, rather than silently returning a<br>short list.<br><br>Ranking has no graph substrate: lesson rows carry no `depends_on` or<br>wikilink edges (SPEC-012 D-04), so retrieval is tag overlap, area match,<br>and summary-token overlap over the flat row list, with `score` as the<br>final tiebreak. A row carrying `escalated` always sorts first: escalation<br>means retrieval already missed or a reader already rejected the lesson |  |

This file is the source of truth the next step translates<br># from - Claude Code itself never reads it directly (see step 5).<br>mkdir -p .claude/hooks<br>cp "$SRC/hooks/hooks.json" .claude/hooks/hooks.json<br><br>echo "Updated: $(ls .claude/agents/*.md \| wc -l) agents, $(ls -d .claude/skills/*/ \| wc -l) skills, $(ls .claude/cli/commands/*.py \| wc -l) CLI modules, hooks.json"<br><br># The hook runs `python3` if present else `python`; verify one exists.<br>if ! command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1 && ! command -v python >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  echo "WARNING: neither python3 nor python on PATH. The vault-sync hook will be a silent no-op (it never blocks writes) until Python 3 is installed."<br>fi<br><br># Write the resolved model policy into the refreshed agents. Rewrites only the<br># model:/effort: frontmatter of the 13 Compass agent files, honoring the<br># project override in .compass/meta/models.yaml; any other file in<br># .claude/agents/ is user-authored and never touched.<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  python3 .claude/cli/compass apply-models<br>elif command -v python >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  python .claude/cli/compass apply-models<br>fi<br>```<br><br>### 5. Register hooks in `.claude/settings.json`<br><br>`.claude/hooks/hooks.json`, just copied, is a manifest - Claude Code loads hooks only from a settings file's `hooks` key or a registered plugin, never from a bare `hooks.json` on disk. Translate the manifest into `.claude/settings.json`, merging into whatever is already there rather than overwriting it: preserve unrelated top-level keys (`permissions`, etc.) and any non-Compass hook entries a project has added by hand. Never write hook registration to `.claude/settings.local.json` - that file is user-owned.<br><br>```bash<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then PYBIN=python3; else PYBIN=python; fi<br>"$PYBIN" - <<'PY'<br>import json<br>from pathlib import Path<br><br>manifest = json.loads(Path(".claude/hooks/hooks.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["hooks"]<br>settings_path = Path(".claude/settings.json")<br>settings = json.loads(settings_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) if settings_path.is_file() else {}<br>hooks = settings.setdefault("hooks", {})<br><br>def is_compass_command(text):<br>    return "compass" in (text or "")<br><br>def is_compass_group(group):<br>    return any(is_compass_command(h.get("command")) for h in group.get("hooks", []))<br><br>def clean_group(group, matcher_override=None):<br>    cleaned = dict(group)<br>    if matcher_override is not None:<br>        cleaned["matcher"] = matcher_override<br>    cleaned["hooks"] = [{k: v for k, v in h.items() if k != "if"} for h in group.get("hooks", [])]<br>    return cleaned<br><br>translated = {}<br>posttool = manifest.get("PostToolUse") or []<br>if posttool:<br>    # The manifest splits PostToolUse three ways (Write/Edit/MultiEdit) because its<br>    # "if" field cannot express boolean OR. The settings schema carries no "if"<br>    # field, so the three collapse into one matcher group.<br>    translated["PostToolUse"] = [clean_group(posttool[0], "Write\|Edit\|MultiEdit")]<br>for event in manifest:<br>    if event != "PostToolUse":<br>        translated[event] = [clean_group(g) for g in manifest[event]]<br><br>for event, new_groups in translated.items():<br>    kept = [g for g in hooks.get(event, []) if not is_compass_group(g)]<br>    hooks[event] = kept + new_groups<br><br>settings_path.write_text(json.dumps(settings, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")<br>print("Hooks registered in .claude/settings.json:", ", ".join(sorted(translated)))<br>PY<br>```<br><br>Re-running this is idempotent: it replaces only the hook groups it previously wrote (any group whose commands mention `compass`) and leaves everything else in `settings.json` untouched.<br><br>### 6. Record the new version<br><br>Update `.compass/meta/plugin.yaml`: set `version` to the clone's version, `commit: $(git -C "$TMP/clone" rev-parse HEAD)`, `installed_at: <today>`, `installed_mode: update`. Preserve `repository:` and `source:` (the latter documents the original install, not the update channel). If `plugin.yaml` does not exist, create it with the fields from [[setup]] step 2's template.<br><br>`commit:` is the sha gate `compass self-update` compares against `git ls-remote` at every session start - after this update lands, the install keeps itself current automatically (SessionStart hook, mandatory per SPEC-020) and this skill is only needed to force a refresh or repair a broken install.<br><br>### 7. Verify with `compass doctor`, clean up, and report<br><br>```bash<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  python3 .claude/cli/compass doctor<br>else<br>  python .claude/cli/compass doctor<br>fi<br>rm -rf "$TMP"<br>```<br><br>Report: version delta (old -> new), counts of files refreshed, whether the python check passed, and doctor's table. A WARN or FAIL row means the update landed only partway - name the failing check and the fix command doctor prints, rather than reporting the update as clean.<br><br>### 8. Nothing to restart<br><br>`.claude/settings.json`'s `hooks` key reloads live in a running session, so the `compass sync`, `compass capture-check`, and `compass capture-signal` hooks registered in step 5 take effect on the next matching event without restarting. Agents are also hot-reloaded: the refreshed `.claude/agents/` and their applied model policy are live immediately. Re-running `compass apply-models` after editing `.compass/meta/models.yaml` also takes effect without a restart. | - **Stop (Recommended):** the install is already current; nothing is copied.<br>- **Force-refresh:** continue to step 4 and copy everything from the clone.<br><br>Never put several questions in one message or one call. If the clone is newer, proceed.<br><br>### 4. Copy everything from the clone (one Bash call)<br><br>```bash<br># Save any shipped file edited by hand before the copy replaces it, and record<br># what this release ships; the clone's own CLI does it, so it runs the new code<br>python "$SRC/cli/compass" self-update --keep-edits "$SRC" \|\| exit 1<br># Agents, rules, skills, slash commands<br>mkdir -p .claude/agents .claude/rules .claude/commands<br>cp "$SRC/templates/agents/"*.md .claude/agents/<br>cp "$SRC/templates/rules/"*.md  .claude/rules/<br>cp "$SRC/templates/commands/"*.md .claude/commands/ 2>/dev/null<br>for d in "$SRC/skills/"*/; do<br>  n=$(basename "$d"); mkdir -p ".claude/skills/$n"; cp "$d"*.md ".claude/skills/$n/"<br>done<br># Remove skills Compass has renamed or retired, so their stale command does not<br># linger. ONLY these named Compass skills are removed - user-authored project<br># skills in .claude/skills/ are never touched. Add a name here when a Compass<br># skill is renamed or deleted (e.g. bootstrap -> setup; tree became the /tree<br># command file).<br>for retired in bootstrap taxonomize tree; do<br>  [ -d ".claude/skills/$retired" ] && rm -rf ".claude/skills/$retired" && echo "removed retired Compass skill: $retired"<br>done<br><br># The compass CLI the PostToolUse/Stop/SubagentStop hooks run<br>rm -rf .claude/cli<br>cp -r "$SRC/cli" .claude/cli<br><br># The Compass Review Obsidian plugin, and its copy into the project's<br># Obsidian vault when Obsidian has opened one (never creates .obsidian/); the<br># VS Code and Cursor extensions are installed only for the project's review editor<br>rm -rf .claude/obsidian<br>cp -r "$SRC/obsidian" .claude/obsidian<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  python3 .claude/cli/compass review --install --selected<br>else<br>  python .claude/cli/compass review --install --selected<br>fi<br><br># Hooks manifest. This file is the source of truth the next step translates<br># from - Claude Code itself never reads it directly (see step 5).<br>mkdir -p .claude/hooks<br>cp "$SRC/hooks/hooks.json" .claude/hooks/hooks.json<br><br>echo "Updated: $(ls .claude/agents/*.md \| wc -l) agents, $(ls -d .claude/skills/*/ \| wc -l) skills, $(ls .claude/cli/commands/*.py \| wc -l) CLI modules, hooks.json"<br><br># The hook runs `python3` if present else `python`; verify one exists.<br>if ! command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1 && ! command -v python >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  echo "WARNING: neither python3 nor python on PATH. The vault-sync hook will be a silent no-op (it never blocks writes) until Python 3 is installed."<br>fi<br><br># Write the resolved model policy into the refreshed agents. Rewrites only the<br># model:/effort: frontmatter of the 13 Compass agent files, honoring the<br># project override in .compass/meta/models.yaml; any other file in<br># .claude/agents/ is user-authored and never touched.<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  python3 .claude/cli/compass apply-models<br>elif command -v python >/dev/null 2>&1; then<br>  python .claude/cli/compass apply-models<br>fi<br>```<br><br>### 4a. Regenerate host materializations<br><br>A machine with DeepSeek Harness on it (detected: `dsh` on PATH, or a harness home directory - no roster field, no question, SPEC-006 D-05) also gets the dsh materializations regenerated from the source just copied - same run, same version, so the hosts never skew. Per project: `.dsh/hooks.json` (absolute-path commands), `.dsh/skills/`, the `AGENTS.md` rules section. Global: the project-agnostic bundle under the harness home (`$DSH_HOME` or `~/.dsh`) plus the dsh-first bootstrap beside it (a plugin snapshot, a bootstrap script, and the hooks file the bundle's second bridge mount reads - a Compass project's first-ever dsh session materializes itself from these), all copied into every profile there, so the user starts dsh in any Compass project and it just works:<br><br>```bash<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then PYBIN=python3; else PYBIN=python; fi<br>"$PYBIN" -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'.claude/cli'); import hostlib<br>if 'dsh' in hostlib.effective_hosts('.compass'):<br>    hostlib.materialize_dsh_hooks('.', '.claude/hooks/hooks.json')<br>    hostlib.materialize_dsh_skills('.', '$SRC/skills')<br>    hostlib.materialize_dsh_instructions('.', '$SRC/templates/rules')<br>    home = hostlib.dsh_home()<br>    hostlib.materialize_dsh_bundle(home, '$SRC')<br>    print('bundle installed into profiles:', hostlib.install_dsh_bundle(home))"<br>```<br><br>The skills and rules build from the clone's shipped files, so a project's own skills never become `compass-*` copies, and each shipped name's local addition (`.compass/meta/local/{skills,rules}/<name>.md`) is appended under its marker as it is built.<br><br>A machine without dsh makes this a no-op - zero dsh-shaped writes.<br><br>### 4b. Re-apply project-local overlays<br><br>```bash<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then PYBIN=python3; else PYBIN=python; fi<br>"$PYBIN" .claude/cli/compass overlay --apply<br>```<br><br>Step 4 replaced every shipped agent, rule, and skill; this appends back the project's own additions from `.compass/meta/local/` (ADR-020). Report what it names, including any ORPHAN line - an overlay whose target is no longer installed. `CLAUDE.md` is never touched by any step here.<br><br>### 5. Register hooks in `.claude/settings.json`<br><br>`.claude/hooks/hooks.json`, just copied, is a manifest - Claude Code loads hooks only from a settings file's `hooks` key or a registered plugin, never from a bare `hooks.json` on disk. Translate the manifest into `.claude/settings.json`, merging into whatever is already there rather than overwriting it: preserve unrelated top-level keys (`permissions`, etc.) and any non-Compass hook entries a project has added by hand. Never write hook registration to `.claude/settings.local.json` - that file is user-owned.<br><br>```bash<br>if command -v python3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then PYBIN=python3; else PYBIN=python; fi<br>"$PYBIN" -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0, '.claude/cli')<br>from commands import self_update<br>self_update.merge_settings('.', '.claude/hooks/hooks.json')<br>print('Hooks registered in .claude/settings.json')"<br>```<br><br>Re-running this is idempotent: it replaces only the hook groups Compass registers (commands the manifest ships or that run `.claude/cli/compass`) and leaves everything else in `settings.json` untouched.<br><br>### 6. Record the new version<br><br>Update `.compass/meta/plugin.yaml`: set `version` to the clone's version, `commit: $(git -C "$TMP/clone" rev-parse HEAD)`, `installed_at: <today>`, `installed_mode: update`. Preserve `repository:` and `source:` (the latter documents the original install, not the update channel). If `plugin.yaml` does not exist, create it with the fields from [[setup]] step 2's template.<br><br>`commit:` is the sha gate `compass self-update` compares against `git ls-remote` at every session start - after this update lands, the install keeps itself current automatically (SessionStart hook, mandatory per SPEC-020) and this skill is only needed to force a refresh or repair a broken install.<br><br>### 7. Verify with `compass doctor`, clean up, and report<br> |  |

| changed | .compass/research/hermes/RESEARCH-hermes-launched-agent-context.md | 223 | c85fc47 | plugin/skills/build/SKILL.md:39 |    - Parallel-safe tasks (non-overlapping `files:`): spawn one builder per task in parallel. Spawn each builder with the Agent tool's `isolation: "worktree"` parameter; that parameter is the only thing that creates the worktree (an `isolation:` key in the agent's frontmatter does nothing). The builder then runs in `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` on branch `worktree-agent-<id>`, and the spawn result names both. Record the (task, branch, worktree) tuple for each. The builder's first action, before writing any code, is to fast-forward its worktree branch to the working branch and confirm the checkpointed test files from step 3 exist; it halts loudly and reports if they do not. Once a builder finishes, spawn the `tester` in post-build mode against its diff, handing it the worktree path so it reads and runs the suite there, never in the orchestrator's checkout. |    - Parallel-safe tasks (non-overlapping `files:`): spawn one builder per task in parallel. Spawn each builder in its own worktree: the Agent tool's `isolation: "worktree"` parameter creates one, and so does `isolation: worktree` in the agent's frontmatter. With `worktree.baseRef: "head"` in the project settings the worktree forks from local HEAD; without it, it forks from the remote default branch and misses unpushed commits. The builder then runs in `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` on branch `worktree-agent-<id>`, and the spawn result names both. Record the (task, branch, worktree) tuple for each. The builder's first action, before writing any code, is to fast-forward its worktree branch to the working branch and confirm the checkpointed test files from step 3 exist; it halts loudly and reports if they do not. Once a builder finishes, spawn the `tester` in post-build mode against its diff, handing it the worktree path so it reads and runs the suite there, never in the orchestrator's checkout. |  |
| changed | .compass/research/hermes/RESEARCH-hermes-launched-agent-context.md | 228 | c85fc47 | plugin/hooks/hooks.json:2 |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. Every entry runs a command, never an agent, so no hook costs a turn of the agent's token budget. PreToolUse runs `compass guard` on Bash, Write, and Edit: it denies a call that would destroy information (a delete or git discard, a Write that empties a file or guts a vault document, an Edit that blanks a long vault passage) through the JSON permission decision, scratch areas exempt. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal`, which writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking.", |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. Every entry runs a command, never an agent, so no hook costs a turn of the agent's token budget. PreToolUse runs `compass guard` on Bash, Write, and Edit: it denies a call that would destroy information (a delete or git discard, a Write that empties a file or guts a vault document, an Edit that blanks a long vault passage) through the JSON permission decision, scratch areas exempt; the entry sets `onFailure: \"block\"` (Claude Code 2.1.295 or later) so a guard that crashes, cannot start, or times out blocks the subagent call instead of letting it through. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal` for each Compass agent under both its bare name (agents installed into a project's .claude/agents) and its plugin-scoped name (`compass:builder` and so on, as agents loaded from a plugin carry), listed side by side in the matcher because a matcher of plain names and pipes is matched exactly; it writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type with any leading `<plugin>:` prefix stripped (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking. The same matcher runs `compass drift --hook`: when HEAD differs from the newest row of the drift log it starts a detached scan of the vault's code references, index lines and catalog rows against git, under a lock file, and returns at once without output. SessionStart also runs `compass session-state` (matcher compact only): after a compaction it injects the session's state block (open tasks, decisions, files touched, uncommitted changes, last test run) as additional context; its shell skips subagent events before Python, matching the quoted `\"agent_id\"` or `\"agent_type\"` key so text the human typed after /compact that mentions those words still reaches the CLI. PreCompact runs the same command on every compaction, manual or auto, and prints the summary instructions that name what the summary must keep.", |  |

| changed | .compass/research/hermes/RESEARCH-hermes-skill-review-gate.md | 153 | e554fa2 | plugin/templates/rules/compass-pipeline.md:18 | **Review in a visual surface.** When a gate's subject is a document and a review surface is configured, the document itself reaches the human, opening with its picture and a "You decide" callout, any guess the agent made marked inline; no walkthrough of it is written in chat. Commit, then run `compass review <file>... --title "<the gate's question>"` in the background; a gate with several documents (a plan and its detail file, a spec and its research) sends them as one review, `--why "<file>=<line>"` saying what to check in each. On `requested <id>`, end the turn with one line naming the document's title, id and commit, and nothing else; `AskUserQuestion` is not called. An approved verdict is the gate's recommended approve option, its notes and `comment` lines steering what follows, and any follow-up question the gate would ask next takes its recommended option unless the notes say otherwise; at approval the "You decide" callout leaves the file, since an approved document states what is. A changes verdict applies the notes, the `comment` lines and the edits (the `edited` lines and `git diff`), then sends the revised document again the same way, opening with a "Since your last review" callout listing what changed. When a review lists several documents, each `file` line's verdict applies to that document alone: an approved document is handled as approved, a changes document is revised and sent again on its own. A reply typed in the terminal while the review is pending runs `compass review --cancel <id>`, and that reply is the answer. On `not configured`, the gate asks through `AskUserQuestion` as the paragraph above describes, and the document's content is shown as plain text. A host with no background wake-up runs the command in the foreground. | **Review in a visual surface.** When a gate's subject is a document and a review surface is configured, the document itself reaches the human, opening with its picture and a "You decide" callout, any guess the agent made marked inline; no walkthrough of it is written in chat. Commit, then run `compass review <file>... --title "<the gate's question>"` in the background; a gate with several documents (a plan and its detail file, a spec and its research) sends them as one review, `--why "<file>=<line>"` saying what to check in each. On `requested <id>`, end the turn with one line naming the document's title, id and commit, and nothing else; `AskUserQuestion` is not called. An approved verdict is the gate's recommended approve option, its notes and `comment` lines steering what follows, and any follow-up question the gate would ask next takes its recommended option unless the notes say otherwise; at approval the "You decide" callout leaves the file, since an approved document states what is. A changes verdict applies the notes, the `comment` lines and the edits (the `edited` lines and `git diff`), then sends the revised document again the same way, opening with a "Since your last review" callout listing what changed. When a review lists several documents, the human answers each one on its own and `compass review` ends on the first answer, printing that document's `file` line and one `still open <id>: ...` line naming the rest. That `file` line's verdict applies to that document alone: an approved document is handled as approved, a changes document is revised and sent again on its own. Then run `compass review --wait <id>` in the background for the documents still open, and repeat until no `still open` line follows. A reply typed in the terminal while the review is pending runs `compass review --cancel <id>`, and that reply is the answer. A `waiting:` line from `compass review` changes nothing for the agent: the review stays open until the human answers. On `not configured`, the gate asks through `AskUserQuestion` as the paragraph above describes, and the document's content is shown as plain text. A host with no background wake-up runs the command in the foreground. |  |

| changed | .compass/research/learning/consolidation-from-sources/AXIS-2-recheck-against-sources.md | 56 | 9a33f39 | plugin/cli/commands/validate.py:1-22 | """`compass validate` - vault integrity check.<br><br>Reports two severities. Errors are things that break machine-readability (a<br>file with no frontmatter, or missing a core field title/type/status) and make<br>the command exit 1. Warnings are advisory (dangling wikilinks, ambiguous<br>wikilinks, unclassified root folders, missing recommended fields, hot-path cap<br>breaches, unreconciled sizing decisions) and do not fail the command -<br>dangling links are valid stubs in an Obsidian vault. Wikilinks resolve through<br>`vaultlib.resolvable_names_map`, the same resolution `sync` emits against,<br>covering every markdown file in the vault including archive/ and custom type<br>dirs. A link name that maps to more than one file is flagged<br>`ambiguous_wikilink`; a root folder that is neither reserved, a marked unit,<br>nor a typed artifact dir is flagged `unclassified_root_folder` - reported for<br>the human, never guessed at. Every unit folder and folder spec on disk is<br>checked against `.compass/meta/sizing-log.yaml` (ADR-011 D-08): a `sizing_id`<br>absent from frontmatter is flagged `sizing_unrecorded`, and a `sizing_id`<br>present but naming no row in the log is flagged `sizing_orphaned_id` -<br>neither ever changes the exit code, since the vault predates the log and<br>every pre-existing shape starts out unrecorded. A block link<br>`[[doc#^id]]` whose target resolves to one file with no line ending in `^id`<br>is flagged `broken_block_link`.<br>""" | """`compass validate` - vault integrity check.<br><br>Reports two severities. Errors are things that break machine-readability (a<br>file with no frontmatter, or missing a core field title/type/status) and make<br>the command exit 1. Warnings are advisory (dangling wikilinks, ambiguous<br>wikilinks, unclassified root folders, missing recommended fields, hot-path cap<br>breaches, unreconciled sizing decisions) and do not fail the command -<br>dangling links are valid stubs in an Obsidian vault. Wikilinks resolve through<br>`vaultlib.resolvable_names_map`, the same resolution `sync` emits against,<br>covering every markdown file in the vault including archive/ and custom type<br>dirs. A link name that maps to more than one file is flagged<br>`ambiguous_wikilink`; a root folder that is neither reserved, a marked unit,<br>nor a typed artifact dir is flagged `unclassified_root_folder` - reported for<br>the human, never guessed at. Every unit folder and folder spec on disk is<br>checked against `.compass/meta/sizing-log.yaml` (ADR-011 D-08): a `sizing_id`<br>absent from frontmatter is flagged `sizing_unrecorded`, and a `sizing_id`<br>present but naming no row in the log is flagged `sizing_orphaned_id` -<br>neither ever changes the exit code, since the vault predates the log and<br>every pre-existing shape starts out unrecorded. A block link<br>`[[doc#^id]]` whose target resolves to one file with no line ending in `^id`<br>is flagged `broken_block_link`. Rewrite verbose bodies - never destroying the original<br><br>For each lesson body longer than 5 lines: distill the rule + reason into <=5 lines, and move the displaced text - the specific instances, the longer wording - into the same file under a `## Record (preserved)` heading below the body. That section is exempt from the 5-line cap, exactly as an archived lesson's `Superseded:` line is: the cap governs the active guidance competing for read budget, and the record below it is cold detail fetched only when someone opens the file. Nothing is dropped; the hot representation shrinks, the data stays.<br><br>Report every rewrite with before/after for the human's audit.<br><br>### 5. Seen arrays are never trimmed<br><br>`seen:` is the recurrence evidence - the dates a lesson proved itself again. It lives only in the lesson file's frontmatter, which no hot-path surface loads, so trimming saves nothing that matters and destroys history that does. Leave every date in place.<br><br>### 6. Archive stale baseline lessons<br><br>For each non-escalated lesson where ALL of these hold:<br><br>- `score == 5` (never been reinforced)<br>- `seen: []` (never recurred)<br>- `updated` was more than 90 days ago<br><br>Move to `.compass/archive/lessons/` (create dir if needed). Set `status: archived` in the file's frontmatter. Update its catalog row to `status: archived`.<br><br>Report every archive for the human's audit.<br><br>### 7. Rebuild lessons-catalog.yaml<br> | <br>A lesson body changes only through a reviewed proposal. This holds for every body change in steps 3 and 4 and for any body change a drift report (`.compass/archive/drift/DRIFT-*.md`) prompts. Write the proposal as one file under `.compass/tmp/proposals/`, one entry per lesson with the old text, the new text, the evidence (the overlapping lines, or the drift finding) and a checkbox. Send it through `compass review <proposal file> --title "Apply these lesson edits?"`. On `not configured`, ask through `AskUserQuestion`, one entry per call. A ticked box applies that entry; an unticked box leaves the lesson as it is; a note beside an entry revises it.<br><br>Draft one entry per matching pair, then send the proposal and, for each ticked merge:<br><br>- Keep the lesson with higher `score` (or earlier `created` if tied)<br>- Sum the `score` values, cap at 10<br>- Union the `tags`<br>- Union the `seen` arrays, keeping every date (step 5)<br>- The losing lesson becomes `status: archived`, its filename is added to the kept lesson's frontmatter as `merged_from: ["LESSON-loser.md"]`<br>- The kept lesson's body stays unless the loser had nuance worth absorbing; the new text then fits the 5-line cap, and every displaced line of the old text moves verbatim into the kept lesson under `## Record (preserved)`<br><br>After each edit run `compass lesson-check <lesson>`; it names the failing line when the body exceeds 5 lines or a body or Record line, a `seen` or `tags` entry, or `score` was lost. Fix the edit until the check passes.<br><br>Report every merge for the human's audit.<br><br>### 4. Rewrite verbose bodies - never destroying the original<br><br>For each lesson body longer than 5 lines, draft a proposal entry (the same proposal file, review and ticks as step 3): the old text, the new text distilling the rule + reason into <=5 lines, the evidence, and a checkbox. For each ticked entry, move the displaced text - the specific instances, the longer wording - verbatim into the same file under a `## Record (preserved)` heading below the body. That section is exempt from the 5-line cap, exactly as an archived lesson's `Superseded:` line is: the cap governs the active guidance competing for read budget, and the record below it is cold detail fetched only when someone opens the file. Nothing is dropped; the hot representation shrinks, the data stays.<br><br>After each edit run `compass lesson-check <lesson>` and fix the edit until it passes.<br><br>Report every rewrite with before/after for the human's audit.<br><br>### 5. Seen arrays are never trimmed<br><br>`seen:` is the recurrence evidence - the dates a lesson proved itself again. It lives only in the lesson file's frontmatter, which no hot-path surface loads, so trimming saves nothing that matters and destroys history that does. Leave every date in place.<br><br>### 6. Archive stale baseline lessons<br><br>For each non-escalated lesson where ALL of these hold:<br><br>- `score == 5` (never been reinforced)<br>- `seen: []` (never recurred)<br>- `updated` was more than 90 days ago<br> |  |
| changed | .compass/research/learning/consolidation-from-sources/AXIS-5-gap-pass.md | 40 | 9a33f39 | bench/overhead/README.md:57-65 | ## Comparing launches<br><br>`token_profile.py PROJECT --by-agent --json` saves one profile per build, with a `by_agent` table: for each `subagent:<type>` the session count, the mean and median weighted tokens per session, every session's weighted tokens, and the share of each source family. `compare.py BEFORE AFTER` pools the sessions of every profile in each folder and prints, per agent type, the launches and the mean weighted tokens in each set, the change, and a 95% bootstrap interval of the change in the mean (2000 resamples, seed fixed). A type seen in one set only shows no change, and an interval needs two sessions on each side. Agent types that Compass does not ship print as `(other)`.<br><br>Pass `--outcomes-before DIR --outcomes-after DIR` to guard the comparison with outcomes. Each folder holds one JSON file per build: a judge verdict (`counts` of goals scored `met`, `partly met`, `not met`) and the build's test result (`recorded.builder_tests`, or a top-level `tests`). When the sets' summed goal counts or test tallies differ, the report prints `outcome differs` and no token comparison.<br><br>## Grading by hand<br><br>`grade.py prepare` picks two builds per arm from the pilot run log (builds that ended human-done first, and one build stopped by a cap for the Compass arm when there is one), copies each as the judge's copy is made (no `.git`, `.compass`, `.claude`, `CLAUDE.md`, no Compass lines in `.gitignore`) without dependency and cache folders, with the text of every file scrubbed of document ids (`SPEC-`, `TASK-`, `PLAN-`, `ADR-`), `.compass`, `.claude`, `CLAUDE.md` and arm labels, and refuses a project with a file name that shows Compass. The copies are `<private root>/grading/grade-1` to `grade-4`, each with a sheet `grade-N.md` beside it listing the frozen goal items. The map from a name to its build is `<private root>/grading-key.json`; it is read only by `score`. Mark each item `met`, `partly met` or `not met` on its `mark gN:` line, write `yes` or `no` on the `keep:` line, then run `grade.py score`. It refuses a sheet with a blank or unknown mark, compares each mark with the judge's score for the same item, writes the section "Agreement with your grading" into the pilot report (counts only, topics by letter) and prints the projects marked keep. The per-item judge scores come from the verdict files under `<private root>/topics/verdicts/`, matched to the run log's judged events by counts and order. | <br>Counting takes each stretch's last `modelUsage` totals (a stretch ends at a `/clear`, which shows as a new `system/init` event) and sums the stretches. The weight of a token is its list price relative to fresh input of the same model: output 5, cache write 1.25, cache read 0.1 (0.05 for the Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 families, 0.025 for Fable 5.1 and Mythos 5.1). A model outside the table adds no weight; its tokens are counted and its id appears in `unpriced_models`.<br><br>## Variant arms<br><br>A variant is Compass with one harness part removed, built under the arm `compass-minus-<part>` (`<part>` is `[a-z][a-z0-9-]*`). Its patch lives in `bench/overhead/variants/<part>.patch`; the first, `post-tester.patch`, removes the post-build and post-merge tester spawns from `plugin/skills/build/SKILL.md` and the "It always runs" line from `plugin/skills/autopilot/SKILL.md`, and keeps the pre-build tester.<br><br>```bash<br>python bench/overhead/variants.py make post-tester      # prints <variants root>/post-tester |  |

| changed | .compass/research/llm-research/RESEARCH-axis-2-host-features.md | 42 | c83c3ca | plugin/hooks/hooks.json:2 |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. Every entry runs a command, never an agent, so no hook costs a turn of the agent's token budget. PreToolUse runs `compass guard` on Bash, Write, and Edit: it denies a call that would destroy information (a delete or git discard, a Write that empties a file or guts a vault document, an Edit that blanks a long vault passage) through the JSON permission decision, scratch areas exempt. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal`, which writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking.", |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. Every entry runs a command, never an agent, so no hook costs a turn of the agent's token budget. PreToolUse runs `compass guard` on Bash, Write, and Edit: it denies a call that would destroy information (a delete or git discard, a Write that empties a file or guts a vault document, an Edit that blanks a long vault passage) through the JSON permission decision, scratch areas exempt; the entry sets `onFailure: \"block\"` (Claude Code 2.1.295 or later) so a guard that crashes, cannot start, or times out blocks the subagent call instead of letting it through. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal` for each Compass agent under both its bare name (agents installed into a project's .claude/agents) and its plugin-scoped name (`compass:builder` and so on, as agents loaded from a plugin carry), listed side by side in the matcher because a matcher of plain names and pipes is matched exactly; it writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type with any leading `<plugin>:` prefix stripped (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking. The same matcher runs `compass drift --hook`: when HEAD differs from the newest row of the drift log it starts a detached scan of the vault's code references, index lines and catalog rows against git, under a lock file, and returns at once without output. SessionStart also runs `compass session-state` (matcher compact only): after a compaction it injects the session's state block (open tasks, decisions, files touched, uncommitted changes, last test run) as additional context; its shell skips subagent events before Python, matching the quoted `\"agent_id\"` or `\"agent_type\"` key so text the human typed after /compact that mentions those words still reaches the CLI. PreCompact runs the same command on every compaction, manual or auto, and prints the summary instructions that name what the summary must keep.", |  |

| changed | .compass/research/llm-research/RESEARCH-axis-2-host-features.md | 196 | c83c3ca | plugin/skills/build/SKILL.md:39 |    - Parallel-safe tasks (non-overlapping `files:`): spawn one builder per task in parallel. Spawn each builder with the Agent tool's `isolation: "worktree"` parameter; that parameter is the only thing that creates the worktree (an `isolation:` key in the agent's frontmatter does nothing). The builder then runs in `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` on branch `worktree-agent-<id>`, and the spawn result names both. Record the (task, branch, worktree) tuple for each. The builder's first action, before writing any code, is to fast-forward its worktree branch to the working branch and confirm the checkpointed test files from step 3 exist; it halts loudly and reports if they do not. Once a builder finishes, spawn the `tester` in post-build mode against its diff, handing it the worktree path so it reads and runs the suite there, never in the orchestrator's checkout. |    - Parallel-safe tasks (non-overlapping `files:`): spawn one builder per task in parallel. Spawn each builder in its own worktree: the Agent tool's `isolation: "worktree"` parameter creates one, and so does `isolation: worktree` in the agent's frontmatter. With `worktree.baseRef: "head"` in the project settings the worktree forks from local HEAD; without it, it forks from the remote default branch and misses unpushed commits. The builder then runs in `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` on branch `worktree-agent-<id>`, and the spawn result names both. Record the (task, branch, worktree) tuple for each. The builder's first action, before writing any code, is to fast-forward its worktree branch to the working branch and confirm the checkpointed test files from step 3 exist; it halts loudly and reports if they do not. Once a builder finishes, spawn the `tester` in post-build mode against its diff, handing it the worktree path so it reads and runs the suite there, never in the orchestrator's checkout. |  |

| changed | .compass/research/llm-research/RESEARCH-axis-2-host-features.md | 217 | c83c3ca | plugin/hooks/hooks.json:2 |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. Every entry runs a command, never an agent, so no hook costs a turn of the agent's token budget. PreToolUse runs `compass guard` on Bash, Write, and Edit: it denies a call that would destroy information (a delete or git discard, a Write that empties a file or guts a vault document, an Edit that blanks a long vault passage) through the JSON permission decision, scratch areas exempt. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal`, which writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking.", |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. Every entry runs a command, never an agent, so no hook costs a turn of the agent's token budget. PreToolUse runs `compass guard` on Bash, Write, and Edit: it denies a call that would destroy information (a delete or git discard, a Write that empties a file or guts a vault document, an Edit that blanks a long vault passage) through the JSON permission decision, scratch areas exempt; the entry sets `onFailure: \"block\"` (Claude Code 2.1.295 or later) so a guard that crashes, cannot start, or times out blocks the subagent call instead of letting it through. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal` for each Compass agent under both its bare name (agents installed into a project's .claude/agents) and its plugin-scoped name (`compass:builder` and so on, as agents loaded from a plugin carry), listed side by side in the matcher because a matcher of plain names and pipes is matched exactly; it writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type with any leading `<plugin>:` prefix stripped (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking. The same matcher runs `compass drift --hook`: when HEAD differs from the newest row of the drift log it starts a detached scan of the vault's code references, index lines and catalog rows against git, under a lock file, and returns at once without output. SessionStart also runs `compass session-state` (matcher compact only): after a compaction it injects the session's state block (open tasks, decisions, files touched, uncommitted changes, last test run) as additional context; its shell skips subagent events before Python, matching the quoted `\"agent_id\"` or `\"agent_type\"` key so text the human typed after /compact that mentions those words still reaches the CLI. PreCompact runs the same command on every compaction, manual or auto, and prints the summary instructions that name what the summary must keep.", |  |
