| changed | .compass/research/pipeline/RESEARCH-control-theory-for-loops.md | 428 | 89fabe2 | plugin/skills/research/SKILL.md:16-21 | \| Skill \| When \| What it spawns \|<br>\|-------\|------\|----------------\|<br>\| `research-codebase` \| "How does X work in the codebase?", "Where is Y handled?", "Trace this flow" \| codebase-locator, codebase-analyzer, pattern-finder \|<br>\| `research-papers` \| "Explain this paper", "What's the prior art for X", an arXiv ID or paper title \| Three researchers (Current / Backward / Forward) plus a reviewer \|<br><br>`codebase <question>` or a plainly code-shaped question (a file path, function name, "where", "trace") dispatches to `research-codebase`. `papers <topic>` or a plainly paper-shaped question (an arXiv ID, paper title, "algorithm", "technique") dispatches to `research-papers`. If neither prefix is given and the question is genuinely ambiguous, ask once whether it is code, papers, or a spec to derive from. Don't reimplement either skill's logic here. | \| Skill \| When \| What it spawns \|<br>\|-------\|------\|----------------\|<br>\| `research-codebase` \| "How does X work in the codebase?", "Where is Y handled?", "Trace this flow" \| codebase-locator, codebase-analyzer, pattern-finder \|<br>\| `research-papers` \| "Explain this paper", "What's the prior art for X", an arXiv ID or paper title \| Three researchers (Current / Backward / Forward) plus a reviewer \|<br><br>`codebase <question>` or a plainly code-shaped question (a file path, function name, "where", "trace") dispatches to `research-codebase`. `papers <topic>` or a plainly paper-shaped question (an arXiv ID, paper title, "algorithm", "technique") dispatches to `research-papers`. If neither prefix is given and the question is genuinely ambiguous, ask once through `AskUserQuestion`, exactly one question per call: "Is this about code, papers, or a spec?". The recommended option comes first, labelled "(Recommended)"; each option is one short line saying what choosing it does ("Code: dispatch to research-codebase", "Papers: dispatch to research-papers", "Spec: derive scope from a spec"); "Other" stays open for free text. Never put several questions in one message or one call. Don't reimplement either skill's logic here. |  |
| changed | .compass/research/pipeline/RESEARCH-control-theory-for-loops.md | 456 | 89fabe2 | plugin/skills/build/SKILL.md:42 | 5. **Fix loop.** If testers report bugs, ask: "Tester found N issues in TASK-NNN. Fix? (yes / skip / abort)". On yes, send the bug diagnosis (not the full task) to the same builder with SendMessage, so the fix lands in its existing worktree and branch; a fresh spawn would open a second worktree that knows nothing of the first. Then run `compass test-checkpoint verify TASK-NNN --tree <worktree path> --expect-checkpoint` against that builder's worktree - without `--tree` the check reads the orchestrator's own checkout, where nothing in the fix loop is changing, and stays silent through the entire cycle. A `modified` result is surfaced to the human before the cycle continues, naming the changed assertion, rather than folded silently into the next one. Spawn the tester in post-build mode again to confirm the fix. Green at fix-loop entry and at every cycle after means **no failures outside `compass test-checkpoint open-ids`** - the checkpointed tests of tasks not yet landed are expected reds, not new bugs. Max 3 cycles per task, then escalate. | 5. **Fix loop.** If testers report bugs, ask: "Tester found N issues in TASK-NNN. Fix? (yes / skip / abort)". On yes, send the bug diagnosis (not the full task) to the same builder with SendMessage, so the fix lands in its existing worktree and branch; a fresh spawn would open a second worktree that knows nothing of the first. Then run `compass test-checkpoint verify TASK-NNN --tree <worktree path> --expect-checkpoint` against that builder's worktree - without `--tree` the check reads the orchestrator's own checkout, where nothing in the fix loop is changing, and stays silent through the entire cycle. A `modified` result is surfaced to the human before the cycle continues, naming the changed assertion, rather than folded silently into the next one. Spawn the tester in post-build mode again to confirm the fix, then run `compass test-checkpoint cycle TASK-NNN --evidence <the run file the tester verified against>` - once at fix-loop entry (cycle 0), then after every confirmation. It prints `cycle N: helpful\|neutral\|harmful` and `escalate: yes\|no`; a harmful cycle ends the loop and goes to the human with the new and vanished tests the command lists. Green at fix-loop entry and at every cycle after means **no failures outside `compass test-checkpoint open-ids`** - the checkpointed tests of tasks not yet landed are expected reds, not new bugs. Max 3 cycles per task, then escalate. |  |
| changed | .compass/research/pipeline/RESEARCH-control-theory-for-loops.md | 581 | 89fabe2 | plugin/cli/commands/overlay.py:1-30 | """`compass overlay` - project-local additions to shipped agents, rules, and<br>skills, re-applied after every update (ADR-020).<br><br>Update replaces `.claude/agents`, `rules`, and `skills` wholesale and now<br>fires at every session start, so an edit made directly to a shipped file<br>dies silently. An overlay is the addition by itself, kept in<br>`.compass/meta/local/` - inside the vault, which update never touches, and<br>version-controlled wherever the project tracks `.compass/`:<br><br>    .compass/meta/local/agents/researcher.md  -> .claude/agents/researcher.md<br>    .compass/meta/local/rules/<name>.md       -> .claude/rules/<name>.md<br>    .compass/meta/local/skills/<name>.md      -> .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md<br><br>Application is append-after-refresh: update copies the shipped file pristine,<br>then this appends the addendum under a provenance comment. The base is<br>regenerated every time, so there is never a previous block to locate - the<br>orphaned-block failure mode of marker splicing cannot occur here, and no<br>anchor can drift. The provenance line doubles as an idempotence guard for a<br>hand-run `--apply` outside the update path.<br><br>Shipped content comes first and the addendum last, so a local instruction<br>that contradicts shipped prose wins by position - the same rule Claude Code's<br>own CLAUDE.md concatenation uses.<br><br>`CLAUDE.md` is never read, written, or moved here or anywhere else in the<br>CLI (SPEC-014 D-02).<br>"""<br><br>import sys<br> | """`compass overlay` - project-local additions to shipped agents, rules, and<br>skills, re-applied after every update (ADR-020).<br><br>Update replaces `.claude/agents`, `rules`, and `skills` wholesale and now<br>fires at every session start, so an edit made directly to a shipped file<br>dies silently. An overlay is the addition by itself, kept in<br>`.compass/meta/local/` - inside the vault, which update never touches, and<br>version-controlled wherever the project tracks `.compass/`:<br><br>    .compass/meta/local/agents/researcher.md  -> .claude/agents/researcher.md<br>    .compass/meta/local/rules/<name>.md       -> .claude/rules/<name>.md<br>    .compass/meta/local/skills/<name>.md      -> .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md<br><br>Application is append-after-refresh: update copies the shipped file pristine,<br>then this appends the addendum under a provenance comment. The provenance<br>line is also the addition's marker. The block runs from the marker line to<br>the end of the target, so a hand-run `--apply` finds its own block, leaves it<br>alone when it matches the local file, and replaces it from the marker to the<br>end when the local file has changed. Text above the marker is never<br>rewritten: a target with CRLF line endings keeps its bytes above the marker<br>and receives the block in its own line ending.<br><br>Each addition is in one of four states:<br><br>    not applied      the target has no block for it<br>    current          the target's block matches the local file<br>    out of date      the target's block differs from the local file<br>    no shipped file  the target does not exist<br><br>An emptied local file leaves a block in the target out of date; applying it |  |

| changed | .compass/research/pipeline/RESEARCH-decision-coverage-impl.md | 124 | 397a324 | plugin/templates/agents/planner.md:91-120 | <br>## Inherited Questions (from spec)<br>[Open questions from the source spec that must be resolved before approval. The planner must not generate new ones - resolve them during planning.]<br>```<br><br>`decisions:` is optional and mirrors `files:` - a list of source-qualified citations (`<doc-stem>/D-NN`) for the decisions a task implements, drawn from the `## Decisions`/`## Decision` bullets of the specs and ADRs this plan depends on. Omit it on tasks that claim nothing.<br><br>Every decision this plan claims must live in some task line's `decisions:` field - detailed or intent, never prose alone. Prose may still discuss a decision, naming it bare (`D-04`) or in backticks; either form is discussion, not a claim, and claims nothing toward coverage. The hazard isn't prose upgrading a task-line claim - a task line always wins over prose - it's prose *manufacturing* one: a citation that appears only in prose, for a decision no task line names anywhere, reads `covered` from that prose alone and can pass `--strict` with nothing committed to build it.<br><br>`lessons:` is optional and mirrors `decisions:` in the same position - a list of catalog lesson filenames (with or without `.md`) the task drew on. `compass lesson-coverage <plan>` audits citations against the catalog; it never gates, so omitting it is always fine.<br><br>`## Wave N elaborated` is the canonical heading for the record section the build flow appends once a wave completes and verifies - you don't author it upfront. An intent line quoted anywhere outside `## Later` - inside a detailed block or a record section - must be fenced or backticked, never live text; a live task line there would claim nothing and silently flip its decisions back to uncovered.<br><br>Write the drafted plan to `.compass/plans/PLAN-NNN-descriptive-name.md` with `status: draft` (PLAN number computed JIT as in step 6) before presenting it - the coverage gate in the next step needs a file to check, and a draft file is expected to still change before approval.<br><br>### 5. Present for approval<br><br>Run `compass coverage <plan>` against the draft file and carry its summary line into the approval message - the human sees the coverage gap, if any, alongside the plan itself.<br><br>```<br>Here is the proposed plan based on [source specs/research]:<br><br>**Goal:** [one sentence]<br>**Wave 1:** N detailed task(s); M later (intent-only) task(s)<br>**Estimated complexity:** [S/M/L overall]<br>**Parallel-safe tasks:** [tasks with non-overlapping files]<br>**Decision coverage:** [`compass coverage` summary line, e.g. "3 trackable decision(s) in 2 source(s): 2 covered, 1 uncovered -> FAIL"]<br><br>[Full plan content]<br> |   - Manual verification: [human checks]<br>  - Entry point: none: <reason the task changes no executable path><br><br>## Risks<br>- [Risk]: [Mitigation]<br><br>## Inherited Questions (from spec)<br>[Open questions from the source spec that must be resolved before approval. The planner must not generate new ones - resolve them during planning.]<br>```<br><br>`decisions:` is optional and mirrors `files:` - a list of source-qualified citations (`<doc-stem>/D-NN`) for the decisions a task implements, drawn from the `## Decisions`/`## Decision` bullets of the specs and ADRs this plan depends on. Omit it on tasks that claim nothing. Every decision this plan claims must live in some task line's `decisions:` field - detailed or intent, never prose alone. Prose may still discuss a decision, naming it bare (`D-04`) or in backticks; either form is discussion, not a claim, and claims nothing toward coverage. The hazard isn't prose upgrading a task-line claim - a task line always wins over prose - it's prose *manufacturing* one: a citation that appears only in prose, for a decision no task line names anywhere, reads `covered` from that prose alone and can pass `--strict` with nothing committed to build it.<br><br>`lessons:` is optional and mirrors `decisions:` in the same position - a list of catalog lesson filenames (with or without `.md`) the task drew on. `compass lesson-coverage <plan>` audits citations against the catalog; it never gates, so omitting it is always fine.<br><br>`## Wave N elaborated` is the canonical heading for the record section the build flow appends once a wave completes and verifies - you don't author it upfront. An intent line quoted anywhere outside `## Later` - inside a detailed block or a record section - must be fenced or backticked, never live text; a live task line there would claim nothing and silently flip its decisions back to uncovered.<br><br>Write the drafted plan to `.compass/plans/PLAN-NNN-descriptive-name.md` with `status: draft` (PLAN number computed JIT as in step 6) before presenting it - the coverage gate in the next step needs a file to check, and a draft file is expected to still change before approval.<br><br>### 5. Present for approval<br><br>Run `compass coverage <plan>` against the draft file and carry its summary line into the approval message - the human sees the coverage gap, if any, alongside the plan itself. With a review surface configured the draft file is the presentation, opening with its picture and "You decide" callout and carrying the coverage line, and the block below is not printed; without a review surface, print:<br><br>```<br>Here is the proposed plan based on [source specs/research]:<br><br>**Goal:** [one sentence]<br>**Wave 1:** N detailed task(s); M later (intent-only) task(s)<br>**Estimated complexity:** [S/M/L overall]<br>**Parallel-safe tasks:** [tasks with non-overlapping files]<br>**Decision coverage:** [`compass coverage` summary line, e.g. "3 trackable decision(s) in 2 source(s): 2 covered, 1 uncovered -> FAIL"] |  |

| changed | .compass/research/test-quality/beyond-test-pass/RESEARCH-integration-and-evidence.md | 29 | adbcd9d | plugin/templates/agents/tester.md:37-60 | ### Pre-build mode<br><br>**1. Read the input contract, nothing else.** Your input is exactly three things: the task body, the plan's acceptance criteria, and the task's automated-verification bullets - the last of these is what supplies the concrete input/expected pairs the equivalence classes get derived from. There is no diff and the implementation does not exist. Do not search for or read implementation code; the whole point of this station is that the misguidance an existing implementation creates is structurally absent here, not merely avoided.<br><br>If the automated-verification bullets are too abstract to derive a concrete equivalence class from, stop. Report it back as a **plan defect** for the orchestrator to amend, rather than guessing at behavior the task never specified.<br><br>**2. Enumerate equivalence classes, then their boundaries.** For each behavior the task promises: name the equivalence classes of input the code is meant to treat identically, then apply the boundary-and-fixture rule to find the edge of each class. Write one test per class plus its boundary.<br><br>**3. Write tests.** Load the `test-design` skill before writing anything - it is the operational admission bar (the docstring convention, the boundary-and-fixture rule, the four classes that never qualify, per-type design guidance) and it governs this station in full. Name the defect class in each test's docstring, using the "Adversarial where:" convention, before writing the test body.<br><br>**4. Run the filter on your own output.** Run `compass test-smells` on the test files you just wrote. Fix every gate finding, or delete the test if it cannot survive the filter - a test that cannot survive the filter was not worth writing, and the checkpoint expects clean entry. Advisory findings are reported alongside the summary, not silently dropped and not auto-fixed.<br><br>**5. Run the suite and record the red evidence.** Run the full suite. Record the exact command and its verbatim failing output:<br><br>```<br>**Command run:** [exact command]<br>**Output:** [actual output]<br>```<br><br>An import or collection error counts as red, but label it as such in the report - a test that has only ever failed to import has not yet been seen to fail for its own reason. The post-build station's `--against-run` check is what later proves it passes for a real one.<br><br>**6. Format.** If the project has a formatter, run it on your test files.<br><br>**7. Report.** List the test files you wrote so the orchestrator can checkpoint them with `compass test-checkpoint record`. See Report format. | ### Pre-build mode<br><br>**1. Format<br><br>If the project has a formatter (prettier, black, gofmt, rustfmt, etc.), run it on the files you changed.<br><br>### 8. Code review<br><br>Spawn a sub-agent with the diff (`git diff`). Charter: flag unused imports, leftover debug statements, inconsistent naming, missing error handling, anything copy-pasted without adaptation. The human may have added domain-specific review rules in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` or `.compass/lessons/`. Apply them.<br><br>Then spawn a second sub-agent for correctness against the spec. It has Read and Grep, and receives the diff, the task line, and the output of `compass requirements <spec> --for-task TASK-NNN --plan <plan>`. Charter:<br>- Per requirement, give a verdict: met, partial, missing, or contradicts, each with file evidence (`file:line`).<br>- Code the requirements do not ask for is surfaced and never removed.<br>- A finding it cannot ground in a file is dropped.<br>- Skip style: the first reviewer owns it. |  |

| changed | .compass/research/test-quality/beyond-test-pass/RESEARCH-integration-and-evidence.md | 112 | adbcd9d | plugin/templates/agents/reviewer.md:42-60 | ### 3. Build the convergence matrix<br><br>```<br>\| Claim \| Agent 1 \| Agent 2 \| Agent 3 \| Agent 4 \| Agent 5 \| Agreement \|<br>\|-------\|---------\|---------\|---------\|---------\|---------\|-----------\|<br>\| X uses pattern A \| YES (high) \| YES (high) \| YES (med) \| YES (high) \| YES (high) \| 100% \|<br>\| Y is deprecated \| YES (med) \| YES (low) \| NO (med) \| YES (med) \| - \| 60% \|<br>\| Z should use lib Q \| YES (high) \| NO (high) \| - \| YES (med) \| NO (med) \| 40% \|<br>```<br><br>### 4. Classify and consolidate<br><br>**Converged (≥80%):** merge into a single finding with high confidence. Use the strongest version of the claim, merge the best evidence.<br><br>**Partial (50-79%):** present the majority position with evidence AND the minority position with evidence. Note what might explain the disagreement. Whether the human wants deeper investigation is a question for the human.<br><br>**Divergent (<50%):** present all positions with full evidence. Do not favor any. Whether the human decides or requests more research is a question for the human.<br><br>You cannot call `AskUserQuestion`. For each such question, stop and return it to the orchestrator with its options and the recommended one marked "(Recommended)", each option one short line saying what choosing it does. The orchestrator asks it through `AskUserQuestion`, exactly one question per call, with "Other" left open for free text; several questions are never batched in one message or one call, and they are asked in order. | ### 3. Build the convergence matrix<br><br>```<br>\| Claim \| Agent 1 \| Agent 2 \| Agent 3 \| Agent 4 \| Agent 5 \| Agreement \|<br>\|-------\|---------\|---------\|---------\|---------\|---------\|-----------\|<br>\| Model (family) \| gpt-5 (gpt) \| claude-sonnet-4-5 (claude) \| claude-opus-4-1 (claude) \| unknown \| claude-sonnet-4-5 (claude) \| \|<br>\| X uses pattern A \| YES (high) \| YES (high) \| YES (med) \| YES (high) \| YES (high) \| 100% \|<br>\| Y is deprecated \| NO (med) \| YES (low) \| YES (med) \| - \| YES (med) \| 60% (3 of 5 share claude) \|<br>\| Z should use lib Q \| YES (high) \| NO (high) \| - \| YES (med) \| NO (med) \| 40% \|<br>```<br><br>The `Model (family)` row comes from each input's `model:` frontmatter, resolved with `compass resolve-model --family`; an input with no `model:` reads `unknown`. When every agent agreeing on a claim shares one family, its Agreement cell adds `(n of m share <family>)`, n being the agreeing agents (all of that family) and m the agents in the matrix. Those claims are counted apart in the Convergence Summary, in a `Same-family agreement` row listing them. The percentage, the counts and the category never change.<br><br>### 4. Classify and consolidate<br><br>**Converged (≥80%):** merge into a single finding with high confidence. Use the strongest version of the claim, merge the best evidence.<br><br>**Partial (50-79%):** present the majority position with evidence AND the minority position with evidence. Note what might explain the disagreement. Whether the human wants deeper investigation is a question for the human.<br> |  |
| changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-graph-engineering-for-compass.md | 74 | 397a324 | plugin/cli/vaultlib.py:283-305 |     unit folder - and classify every artifact markdown file.<br><br>    Each record is a dict with: `path`, `type_dir` (bare type dir name, e.g.<br>    `specs` even inside a unit), `kind` (`flat` \| `folder-index` \| `child`),<br>    `rel` (POSIX path within the type dir), `name` (wikilink identity;<br>    vault-relative for unit artifacts), `depth` (folder nesting level), and<br>    `unit` (owning unit folder name, None for root artifacts). A unit's own<br>    `index.md` is the unit's marker, not an artifact, and is not scanned.<br>    """<br>    vault_root = Path(vault_root)<br>    layout = classify_root_dirs(vault_root)<br>    records = []<br>    for type_dir in layout["type_dirs"]:<br>        records.extend(_scan_type_dir(vault_root / type_dir, type_dir, None))<br>    for unit in layout["units"]:<br>        unit_dir = vault_root / unit<br>        for type_dir in classify_root_dirs(unit_dir)["type_dirs"]:<br>            records.extend(_scan_type_dir(unit_dir / type_dir, type_dir, unit))<br>    return records<br><br><br>def resolvable_names_map(vault_root):<br>    """Every name a wikilink may resolve to, mapped to the vault-relative |         paragraphs.append(" ".join(current))<br>    return "\n".join(paragraphs)<br><br><br>def parse_frontmatter_text(text):<br>    """Parse a YAML frontmatter block from `text`.<br><br>    Returns `(data, error)`. `error` is None on success, or a short string<br>    when the block is missing or unterminated. Handles the frontmatter subset<br>    the vault uses: scalar `key: value`, inline `key: [a, b]`, block lists<br>    of `- item` under a key, and `key: >` / `key: \|` block scalars (with<br>    chomping indicators), whose indented continuation lines fold into the<br>    value. The closing fence is an unindented `---` line; an indented one<br>    is block-scalar content. Never raises on malformed content; unparseable<br>    lines are skipped.<br>    """<br>    lines = text.splitlines()<br>    if not lines or lines[0].strip() != "---":<br>        return {}, "no frontmatter"<br>    end = None<br>    for i in range(1, len(lines)):<br>        if lines[i].rstrip() == "---":<br>            end = i |  |
| changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-graph-engineering-for-compass.md | 80 | 397a324 | plugin/cli/decisionslib.py:1-29 | """Decision extraction from vault documents.<br><br>A decision is a `- **D-NN:** text` bullet (continuation lines indented under<br>it) inside a decisions section: a two- or three-hash heading whose first word<br>is Decision/Decisions, case-insensitive, any suffix - `## Decision`,<br>`## Decisions (made by the human)`, `### decisions`.<br><br>`extract_decisions` returns one of three outcomes, so a format mismatch can<br>never pass as a clean "nothing to check":<br><br>- `parsed`: at least one decision extracted and every decision-shaped bullet<br>  understood.<br>- `none-present`: no decision signals anywhere in the document.<br>- `could-not-parse`: decision-shaped content the grammars could not read.<br>  One malformed `- **D-` bullet poisons the whole extraction even when other<br>  bullets parsed, because silently dropping one decision is the exact failure<br>  this module exists to prevent. With a decisions section found but zero<br>  bullets extracted, a bare `D-` token, an ID-shaped bold-lead-in bullet, or<br>  an unterminated code fence is evidence of real entries the parser could<br>  not read. A `D-NN` token qualified by a preceding document stem (`SPEC-`,<br>  `ADR-`, `PLAN-`, `RESEARCH-`, joined by a space or a slash) or sitting<br>  inside a `[[wikilink]]` cites another document's decision and is not<br>  evidence there.<br><br>Opt-outs: an `[informational]` or `[deferred]` bracket tag on the bullet, or<br>placement under a discretion subheading, marks the decision non-trackable.<br>It is still parsed and recorded, never gate-checked.<br><br>Fenced code blocks and inline code spans are never decision content, so a | """Decision extraction from vault documents.<br><br>A decision is a `- **D-NN:** text` bullet (continuation lines indented under<br>it) inside a decisions section: a two- or three-hash heading whose first word<br>is Decision/Decisions, case-insensitive, any suffix - `## Decision`,<br>`## Decisions (made by the human)`, `### decisions`.<br><br>`extract_decisions` returns one of three outcomes, so a format mismatch can<br>never pass as a clean "nothing to check":<br><br>- `parsed`: at least one decision extracted and every decision-shaped bullet<br>  understood.<br>- `none-present`: no decision signals anywhere in the document.<br>- `could-not-parse`: decision-shaped content the grammars could not read.<br>  One malformed `- **D-` bullet poisons the whole extraction even when other<br>  bullets parsed, because silently dropping one decision is the exact failure<br>  this module exists to prevent. | changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-taxonomy-for-unambiguous-placement.md | 315 | 002a0cf | plugin/skills/consolidate/SKILL.md:164 | Execute with the existing commands - `compass promote --apply`, `compass make-unit --apply`, `git mv` into folder specs - so every shape change lands in the sizing log where those commands write it. Wikilinks keep resolving (bare stems stay unique or become path-qualified per the linking rule); run `compass validate` after each domain and stop on any broken link. | changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-taxonomy-for-unambiguous-placement.md | 323 | 002a0cf | plugin/skills/obsidian/SKILL.md:144 | **The parent holds the decisions shared by every child; a child exists to diverge on something the parent left open.** Divergence sharing no root decision with the parent is a sibling spec, not a child (ADR-011 D-06). |  |  |
| changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-taxonomy-for-unambiguous-placement.md | 323 | 002a0cf | plugin/skills/obsidian/SKILL.md:169-193 | ### Folder-level index.md (the warm tier branch summary)<br><br>A folder's `index.md` is the parent spec body PLUS a RAPTOR-style summary of its children. Hold decisions and concerns that stay at this level. | changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-taxonomy-for-unambiguous-placement.md | 331 | 002a0cf | plugin/cli/commands/sync.py:147-190 | def _sync_index(vault_root, records):<br>    """Append missing entries: one section per root type dir, one section per<br>    unit titled from the unit's `index.md`. An artifact counts as already indexed when any existing<br>    wikilink in its section resolves to the artifact's file. The index's Lessons section is a pointer.<br>        if record["type_dir"] == "lessons":<br>            continue<br>        # The root index speaks in top-level entries (ADR-021): a folder<br>        # artifact's line, with its child count, is the pointer to its whole<br>        # subtree. Children of a folder artifact live inside it and resolve<br>        # by wikilink; listing them here would price every domain member<br>        # back onto the hot path. A loose nested doc (plain grouping<br>        # subfolder, no index.md above it) has no folder line pointing at<br>        # it, so it stays listed.<br>        if record["depth"] > 0 and not vaultlib.is_loose_nested(<br>            record["path"], record["kind"]<br>        ):<br>            continue |     label = f"[[{_link_name(record)}]]"<br>    if record["kind"] == "folder-index":<br>        return f"{indent}- {label} (folder, {child_count} children) - {summary}"<br>    return f"{indent}- {label} - {summary}"<br><br><br>def _child_count(folder_record, records):<br>    prefix = folder_record["name"] + "/"<br>    return sum(<br>        1 for r in records<br>        if r["name"].startswith(prefix) and "/" not in r["name"][len(prefix):]<br>    )<br><br><br>def _sync_children_counts(records):<br>    """Rewrite `children_count:` in each folder artifact's own index.md to<br>    the number of its direct children, the count the root index line shows<br>    for the same folder. | changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-taxonomy-for-unambiguous-placement.md | 357 | 002a0cf | plugin/skills/obsidian/SKILL.md:217-232 | ### Bare stems vs path-qualified links<br><br>Numbering is local per unit, so `SPEC-001-...` can exist at the root and inside any unit - bare stems are genuinely ambiguous across units. The link form depends on where the target lives:<br><br>\| Target \| Link form \| Example \|<br>\|--------\|-----------\|---------\|<br>\| Root flat artifact \| bare stem \| `[[SPEC-004-mechanical-work-off-the-agent-budget]]` \|<br>\| Root folder spec \| folder name \| `[[SPEC-002-tile-editor]]` \|<br>\| Unit artifact \| path-qualified `<unit>/<type-dir>/<stem>` \| `[[compass-cli/specs/SPEC-001-cli-contract]]` \|<br>\| Folder spec inside a unit \| folder's vault-relative path \| `[[compass-cli/specs/SPEC-002-brush-system]]` \|<br><br>Always author unit-artifact links path-qualified. `compass sync` emits exactly these forms in the root index, and `compass validate` warns `ambiguous_wikilink` when a bare stem resolves to more than one file; the path-qualified form resolves to exactly one.<br><br>### Where a new artifact goes<br><br>Before creating an artifact, resolve its destination root: if the work belongs to an existing unit (its spec, plan, or research lives under `.compass/<unit>/`), create the artifact in that unit's type dir, number it with `compass next-num <type> <unit>`, and link it path-qualified. Otherwise create it in the root type dir with a bare-stem link. Detection of root artifact sets that have outgrown the flat layout is mechanical (`compass unit-check` reports candidates when 3+ artifact types trace to one spec); promotion is the human's decision. |  |  |

skip it.<br>                continue<br>            kind = "folder-index"<br>            name = "/".join(parts[:-1])<br>            # A folder spec renders at the depth of its folder, which sits<br>            # one level above its own `index.md` file.<br>            depth = len(parts) - 2<br>        elif len(parts) == 1:<br>            kind = "flat"<br>            name = path.stem<br>            depth = 0<br>        else:<br>            kind = "child"<br>            loose = is_loose_nested(path, kind)<br>            if loose:<br>                name = "/".join([type_dir] + list(parts[:-1]) + [path.stem])<br>            else:<br>                name = "/".join(list(parts[:-1]) + [path.stem])<br>            depth = len(parts) - 1<br>        if unit is not None:<br>            if kind == "child" and loose:<br>                name = f"{unit}/{name}"<br>            else:<br>                name = f"{unit}/{type_dir}/{name}"<br>        records.append({<br>            "path": path,<br>            "type_dir": type_dir,<br>            "kind": kind,<br>            "rel": str(rel).replace("\\", "/"),<br>            "name": name,<br>            "depth": depth,<br>            "unit": unit,<br>        })<br>    return records | <br><br>def _split_inline_list(inner):<br>    """Split the inside of an inline `[a, b, c]` list, respecting quotes."""<br>    items, current, quote = [], "", None<br>    for char in inner:<br>        if quote:<br>            current += char<br>            if char == quote:<br>                quote = None<br>        elif char in "\"'":<br>            quote = char<br>            current += char<br>        elif char == ",":<br>            items.append(current)<br>            current = ""<br>        else:<br>            current += char<br>    if current.strip():<br>        items.append(current)<br>    return [_scalar(item) for item in items]<br><br><br>def _block_scalar(style, block_lines):<br>    """Value of a `>` folded or `\|` literal block scalar. This half shrinks structural weight: a root index listing every artifact flat. The remedy is grouping into domains - "the same way you organize mathematics into subdomains", recursively - so the root index says one line per broad area (ADR-021: children of a folder artifact are not listed at the root; the folder line with its child count is the pointer).<br><br>### S1. Inventory<br><br>Glob every flat root artifact in scope (specs and research first; plans mirror their specs). Read frontmatter (title, area, tags, summary, status) and record outbound wikilinks. `compass graph hubs` and `compass graph impact` give the link structure cheaply.<br><br>### S2. Propose domains<br><br>Group artifacts that share tags, a title theme, or a dependency neighborhood under a proposed domain: related flat specs fold as children into a folder spec (`compass promote` on the natural parent, or a new parent folder); research docs fold into a folder named for the domain; a domain that is a genuine ongoing workstream is a unit (`compass make-unit`). Subdomains nest inside domains at any depth. Spawn an agent sub-task for the grouping judgment when the corpus is large - it is heuristic work and benefits from a fresh context.<br><br>While proposing, also flag tag-vocabulary repairs (synonyms to one canonical form, tags on a single artifact, artifacts with no tags) - the facets are what multi-domain retrieval uses.<br><br>### S3. Present the proposal as a diff, and stop<br><br>One block per proposed domain: the folder to create, the files that move into it (exact `git mv` paths), the one index line that replaces their N lines, and the rationale. Domains are the human's knowledge's shape - only his approval moves files. Without approval this pass is read-only.<br><br>### S4. Apply after approval<br><br>Execute with the existing commands - `compass promote --apply`, `compass make-unit --apply`, `git mv` into folder specs - so every shape change lands in the sizing log where those commands write it. Wikilinks keep resolving (bare stems stay unique or become path-qualified per the linking rule); run `compass validate` after each domain and stop on any broken link. Nothing is deleted, ever: grouping moves files.<br><br>### S5. Report<br><br>Domains created, files moved per domain, index lines before -> after, validate clean. | ### Archived (baseline + stale)<br>- [[LESSON-old]] - score 5, no recurrence, last updated 2026-01-15<br><br>### Records preserved<br>- [[LESSON-qux]] - displaced body text moved under `## Record (preserved)`<br><br>### Caps after consolidation<br>- index.md: <N> lines (cap 200)<br>- catalog: <N> lines (cap 200)<br>- lessons/: <N> active files (cap 50)<br>```<br><br><br>## Structure pass (consolidation is also taxonomy)<br><br>The lessons half above shrinks textual redundancy. This half shrinks structural weight: a root index listing every artifact flat. The remedy is grouping into domains - "the same way you organize mathematics into subdomains", recursively - so the root index says one line per broad area (ADR-021: children of a folder artifact are not listed at the root; the folder line with its child count is the pointer).<br><br>### S1. Inventory<br><br>First collect the hints: run `compass placement --hints`, which reports every artifact with a pending `taxonomy_hint:` across `specs/`, `research/`, `decisions/`, and `lessons/`, each with its placement candidates and the current path of every artifact its hint names. Each is a placement judgment recorded at creation by the agent that understood the artifact best, and together they are this pass's work queue. Acting on one clears it - the move happens, or the placement is confirmed fine - and the frontmatter line is removed either way.<br><br>Glob every flat root artifact in scope: specs, research, decisions, and lessons all taxonomize the same way (SPEC-022 D-12) - ADRs usually reuse the spec domains since every ruling resolves a fork inside one; lessons split by the craft each teaches; plans mirror their specs. Read frontmatter (title, area, tags, summary, status) and record outbound wikilinks. `compass graph hubs` and `compass graph impact` give the link structure cheaply.<br><br>### S2. Propose domains<br> |  |

| changed | .compass/research/vault-structure/RESEARCH-taxonomy-for-unambiguous-placement.md | 460 | 002a0cf | plugin/skills/spec/SKILL.md:65 | Before creating the spec file, resolve its destination per the `obsidian` skill's "Where a new artifact goes" rule - the owning unit's `specs/` dir, or the root. Every spec is a folder: `.compass/specs/SPEC-NNN-descriptive-name/index.md`, NNN from `compass next-num spec`. A child spec is created the same way inside its parent's folder, at any depth - `compass next-num spec <parent-path>` numbers it locally. Required sections: Problem and Desired Outcome. Optional: User Scenarios, Constraints, Decisions, Non-Goals, Risks, Open Questions. Omit any optional section if empty - don't include a heading with no content. | An approved move runs in this step, so no folder is left holding only its index. A child: `compass promote <parent>` turns the parent into `SPEC-NNN-name/index.md`, then `compass move <draft> <parent-folder>` files the spec inside it. A pair: `compass make-domain specs/<path>` creates the domain, then `compass move` files both specs into it. A candidate parent or pair is always proposed; the spec stays shallow with `taxonomy_hint: "<one line: where this might belong>"` in the frontmatter only when the human declines or nothing fits. Validate surfaces pending hints on every run, and consolidate consumes them. |  |

| changed | .compass/research/whole-overhead/RESEARCH-axis-3-simulated-human.md | 89 | c8b465b | plugin/templates/rules/compass-pipeline.md:16-18 | Ask through `AskUserQuestion`, exactly one question per call: the options are a numbered list, the recommended one first and labelled "(Recommended)", each one short line saying what choosing it does, the free-text "Other" left open, and a reply of just a number picks that option. The tool numbers its options, so labels carry none; a choice shown as text, as on a host without the tool, is written as `1.`, `2.`, `3.` lines. Several questions are never batched in one message or one call; the next question waits for this answer. A subagent cannot call `AskUserQuestion`: it stops and returns the question to the orchestrator, its options numbered and the recommended one marked, and the orchestrator asks it the same way.<br><br>**Review in a visual surface.** When a gate's subject is a document and a review surface is configured, the document itself reaches the human, opening with its picture and a "You decide" callout, any guess the agent made marked inline; no walkthrough of it is written in chat. Commit, then run `compass review <file>... --title "<the gate's question>"` in the background; a gate with several documents (a plan and its detail file, a spec and its research) sends them as one review, `--why "<file>=<line>"` saying what to check in each. On `requested <id>`, end the turn with one line naming the document's title, id and commit, and nothing else; `AskUserQuestion` is not called. An approved verdict is the gate's recommended approve option, its notes and `comment` lines steering what follows, and any follow-up question the gate would ask next takes its recommended option unless the notes say otherwise; at approval the "You decide" callout leaves the file, since an approved document states what is. A changes verdict applies the notes, the `comment` lines and the edits (the `edited` lines and `git diff`), then sends the revised document again the same way, opening with a "Since your last review" callout listing what changed. When a review lists several documents, each `file` line's verdict applies to that document alone: an approved document is handled as approved, a changes document is revised and sent again on its own. A reply typed in the terminal while the review is pending runs `compass review --cancel <id>`, and that reply is the answer. On `not configured`, the gate asks through `AskUserQuestion` as the paragraph above describes, and the document's content is shown as plain text. A host with no background wake-up runs the command in the foreground. | Ask through `AskUserQuestion`, exactly one question per call: the options are a numbered list, the recommended one first and labelled "(Recommended)", each one short line saying what choosing it does, the free-text "Other" left open, and a reply of just a number picks that option. The tool numbers its options, so labels carry none; a choice shown as text, as on a host without the tool, is written as `1.`, `2.`, `3.` lines. Several questions are never batched in one message or one call; the next question waits for this answer. A subagent cannot call `AskUserQuestion`: it stops and returns the question to the orchestrator, its options numbered and the recommended one marked, and the orchestrator asks it the same way.<br><br>**Review in a visual surface.** When a gate's subject is a document and a review surface is configured, the document itself reaches the human, opening with its picture and a "You decide" callout, any guess the agent made marked inline; no walkthrough of it is written in chat. Commit, then run `compass review <file>... --title "<the gate's question>"` in the background; a gate with several documents (a plan and its detail file, a spec and its research) sends them as one review, `--why "<file>=<line>"` saying what to check in each. On `requested <id>`, end the turn with one line naming the document's title, id and commit, and nothing else; `AskUserQuestion` is not called. An approved verdict is the gate's recommended approve option, its notes and `comment` lines steering what follows, and any follow-up question the gate would ask next takes its recommended option unless the notes say otherwise; at approval the "You decide" callout leaves the file, since an approved document states what is. A changes verdict applies the notes, the `comment` lines and the edits (the `edited` lines and `git diff`), then sends the revised document again the same way, opening with a "Since your last review" callout listing what changed. When a review lists several documents, the human answers each one on its own and `compass review` ends on the first answer, printing that document's `file` line and one `still open <id>: ...` line naming the rest. That `file` line's verdict applies to that document alone: an approved document is handled as approved, a changes document is revised and sent again on its own. Then run `compass review --wait <id>` in the background for the documents still open, and repeat until no `still open` line follows. A reply typed in the terminal while the review is pending runs `compass review --cancel <id>`, and that reply is the answer. A `waiting:` line from `compass review` changes nothing for the agent: the review stays open until the human answers. On `not configured`, the gate asks through `AskUserQuestion` as the paragraph above describes, and the document's content is shown as plain text. A host with no background wake-up runs the command in the foreground. |  |

| changed | .compass/research/whole-overhead/RESEARCH-axis-3-simulated-human.md | 107 | c8b465b | plugin/cli/reviewlib.py:1-50 | """The review protocol between Compass and any visual md interface.<br><br>A review is two JSON files in the Compass vault's `tmp/review/`:<br><br>- `<id>.request.json`, written here:<br>  `{"id", "surface", "file", "title", "return", "commit"}`. `surface` names<br>  the interface the request went to; `file` is the reviewed document's path<br>  relative to that surface's root, forward slashes. A request for several<br>  files also carries `files`, `[{"file", "why"}]` in the order given, `why`<br>  being a one-line reason or null and `files[0].file` equal to `file`;<br>  `session`, the id of the session that asked (empty when unknown); and<br>  `project`, the name of the folder holding the vault. `return` asks the<br>  surface to step aside after the verdict, set when it did not have focus<br>  as the request was made. `commit` is the full sha of the git HEAD the<br>  document was committed at when the request went out; the changes made<br>  during the review are everything that differs from it.<br>- `<id>.verdict.json`, written by the surface on the human's answer:<br>  `{"id", "file", "verdict": "approved"\|"changes", "notes", "at"}`, plus<br>  three optional lists: `comments`, `{"file", "note"}` entries for notes the<br>  human left on other files while reviewing, `edited`, the paths of other<br>  files the human changed, and `files`, `{"file", "read", "note", "verdict"}`<br>  entries marking each listed file read or unread (`read` a boolean, `note` a<br>  string or absent). In a several-file review each entry's `verdict` is<br>  `"approved"` or `"changes"`, that file's own answer, and the top-level<br>  `verdict` is `"approved"` when every file is approved, else `"changes"`. An<br>  entry without a `verdict` takes the top-level one.<br><br>- `<id>.changes.diff`, written here beside the request when the review lists<br>  a document an earlier review in the vault already listed: `git diff <sha><br>  -- <file>` for each listed file whose content differs from the commit its<br>  own latest earlier review was sent at (`commit` in that request), run from<br>  the repository top and joined in listed order. A first review, and a file<br>  that equals what the earlier review showed, add nothing; with nothing to<br>  add no file is written. It is written before the request, and removing the<br>  request removes it.<br><br>- `<id>.seen`, an empty file written by the surface beside the request when it<br>  takes the request up. Its presence tells `compass review` the add-on is<br>  loaded and has the request; a request still without one after the<br>  acknowledgement window is withdrawn. Removing the request removes it, and<br>  writing a request removes every `.seen` older than `ORPHAN_SECONDS` (an<br>  hour) whose request is gone. It does not make the request answered.<br><br>A request with no verdict is pending; while it is pending every file it<br>lists belongs to the human. Removing a pending request's file withdraws it: its<br>waiter ends and the surface drops the review. The surfaces themselves live<br>in `review_surfaces`.<br>"""<br><br>import json | """The review protocol between Compass and any visual md interface.<br><br>A review is two JSON files in the Compass vault's `tmp/review/`:<br><br>- `<id>.request.json`, written here:<br>  `{"id", "surface", "file", "title", "return", "commit"}`. `surface` names<br>  the interface the request went to; `file` is the reviewed document's path<br>  relative to that surface's root, forward slashes. A request for several<br>  files also carries `files`, `[{"file", "why"}]` in the order given, `why`<br>  being a one-line reason or null and `files[0].file` equal to `file`;<br>  `session`, the id of the session that asked (empty when unknown); and<br>  `project`, the name of the folder holding the vault. `return` asks the<br>  surface to step aside after the verdict, set when it did not have focus<br>  as the request was made. `commit` is the full sha of the git HEAD the<br>  document was committed at when the request went out; the changes made<br>  during the review are everything that differs from it.<br>- `<id>.verdict.json`, written by the surface on the human's answer:<br>  `{"id", "file", "verdict": "approved"\|"changes", "notes", "at"}`, plus<br>  three optional lists: `comments`, `{"file", "note"}` entries for notes the<br>  human left on other files while reviewing, `edited`, the paths of other<br>  files the human changed, and `files`, `{"file", "read", "note", "verdict"}`<br>  entries marking each listed file read or unread (`read` a boolean, `note` a<br>  string or absent). In a several-file review each entry's `verdict` is<br>  `"approved"` or `"changes"`, that file's own answer, and the top-level<br>  `verdict` is `"approved"` when every file is approved, else `"changes"`. An<br>  entry without a `verdict` takes the top-level one.<br><br>- `<id>.verdict.<n>.json`, written by the surface when the human answers some<br>  documents of a several-file review and leaves others open: the same shape<br>  as `<id>.verdict.json`, its `files` listing only the answered documents,<br>  each entry also carrying `index`, the document's 0-based position in the<br>  request's `files`; `<n>` is the lowest `index` it carries and the top-level<br>  `verdict` is `"approved"` when every entry is approved, else `"changes"`. A<br>  document is answered once; the request is answered when `<id>.verdict.json`<br>  exists or the `<n>` files together cover every listed document. A waiter<br>  that hands such an answer on writes the empty `<id>.verdict.<n>.taken`<br>  beside it, so a later `compass review --wait <id>` takes up the next<br>  answer. A document the human has answered is no longer under review.<br><br>- `<id>.changes.diff`, written here beside the request when the review lists<br>  a document an earlier review in the vault already listed: `git diff <sha><br>  -- <file>` for each listed file whose content differs from the commit its<br>  own latest earlier review was sent at (`commit` in that request), run from<br>  the repository top and joined in listed order. A first review, and a file<br>  that equals what the earlier review showed, add nothing; with nothing to<br>  add no file is written. Its presence tells `compass review` the add-on is |  |
| changed | .compass/research/whole-overhead/RESEARCH-axis-3-simulated-human.md | 107 | c8b465b | plugin/cli/commands/review.py:294-318 | def _wait(request, request_path, documents, timeout, root, vault, surface_root, surface):<br>    target = reviewlib.verdict_path(request_path)<br>    claim = reviewlib.seen_path(request_path)<br>    started = time.monotonic()<br>    deadline = None if timeout is None else started + timeout<br>    claimed = False<br>    while deadline is None or time.monotonic() < deadline:<br>        if target.exists():<br>            verdict = reviewlib.read_json(target)<br>            if reviewlib.valid_verdict(verdict, request):<br>                _print_answer(request, verdict, documents, root, vault, surface_root)<br>                return 0<br>        if not Path(request_path).exists():<br>            print(f"withdrawn {request['id']}")<br>            return 1<br>        claimed = claimed or claim.exists()<br>        if not claimed and time.monotonic() - started >= ACK_SECONDS:<br>            if not claim.exists() and reviewlib.cancel(vault, request["id"]):<br>                print(f"not configured: {surface.label} did not pick up review {request['id']} "<br>                      f"within {ACK_SECONDS:g} s; Then run `compass review --wait <id>` in the background for the documents still open, and repeat until no `still open` line follows. A reply typed in the terminal while the review is pending runs `compass review --cancel <id>`, and that reply is the answer. A `waiting:` line from `compass review` changes nothing for the agent: the review stays open until the human answers. On `not configured`, the gate asks through `AskUserQuestion` as the paragraph above describes, and the document's content is shown as plain text. A host with no background wake-up runs the command in the foreground. |  |
| changed | .compass/research/whole-overhead/RESEARCH-axis-6-build-success.md | 73 | c8b465b | plugin/templates/agents/tester.md:49-56 | **5. Run the suite and record the red evidence.** Run the full suite. Record the exact command and its verbatim failing output:<br><br>```<br>**Command run:** [exact command]<br>**Output:** [actual output]<br>```<br><br>An import or collection error counts as red, but label it as such in the report - a test that has only ever failed to import has not yet been seen to fail for its own reason. The post-build station's `--against-run` check is what later proves it passes for a real one. | **5. Run the suite and record the red evidence.** Run the full suite with the runner's verbose output, one line per test (`pytest -v`, `python -m unittest -v`, `node --test` TAP), so each test's own status can be read from the saved run. Record the exact command and its verbatim failing output:<br><br>```<br>**Command run:** [exact command]<br>**Output:** [actual output]<br>```<br><br>An import or collection error counts as red, but label it as such in the report - a test that has only ever failed to import has not yet been seen to fail for its own reason; `compass test-checkpoint record --red-evidence <file>` stores each test's kind (`fail`, `error`, `collection`, `passed`, `absent`) from the saved output, and `compass ablate` reports a collection-only red that stays green or vanishes without the code as `unproven:`, never a finding. The post-build station's `--against-run` check is what later proves it passes for a real one. |  |

| changed | .compass/research/whole-overhead/RESEARCH-compass-context-launcher-options.md | 119 | c85fc47 | plugin/skills/build/SKILL.md:39 |    - Parallel-safe tasks (non-overlapping `files:`): spawn one builder per task in parallel. Spawn each builder with the Agent tool's `isolation: "worktree"` parameter; that parameter is the only thing that creates the worktree (an `isolation:` key in the agent's frontmatter does nothing). The builder then runs in `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` on branch `worktree-agent-<id>`, and the spawn result names both. Record the (task, branch, worktree) tuple for each. The builder's first action, before writing any code, is to fast-forward its worktree branch to the working branch and confirm the checkpointed test files from step 3 exist; it halts loudly and reports if they do not. Once a builder finishes, spawn the `tester` in post-build mode against its diff, handing it the worktree path so it reads and runs the suite there, never in the orchestrator's checkout. |    - Parallel-safe tasks (non-overlapping `files:`): spawn one builder per task in parallel. Spawn each builder in its own worktree: the Agent tool's `isolation: "worktree"` parameter creates one, and so does `isolation: worktree` in the agent's frontmatter. With `worktree.baseRef: "head"` in the project settings the worktree forks from local HEAD; without it, it forks from the remote default branch and misses unpushed commits. The builder then runs in `.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` on branch `worktree-agent-<id>`, and the spawn result names both. Record the (task, branch, worktree) tuple for each. The builder's first action, before writing any code, is to fast-forward its worktree branch to the working branch and confirm the checkpointed test files from step 3 exist; it halts loudly and reports if they do not. Once a builder finishes, spawn the `tester` in post-build mode against its diff, handing it the worktree path so it reads and runs the suite there, never in the orchestrator's checkout. |  |

| changed | .compass/research/whole-overhead/checking-cost/RESEARCH-axis-3-part-by-part-rebaseline.md | 66 | 08ec36b | plugin/hooks/hooks.json:2 |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. Every entry runs a command, never an agent, so no hook costs a turn of the agent's token budget. PreToolUse runs `compass guard` on Bash, Write, and Edit: it denies a call that would destroy information (a delete or git discard, a Write that empties a file or guts a vault document, an Edit that blanks a long vault passage) through the JSON permission decision, scratch areas exempt; the entry sets `onFailure: \"block\"` (Claude Code 2.1.295 or later) so a guard that crashes, cannot start, or times out blocks the subagent call instead of letting it through. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal` for each Compass agent under both its bare name (agents installed into a project's .claude/agents) and its plugin-scoped name (`compass:builder` and so on, as agents loaded from a plugin carry), listed side by side in the matcher because a matcher of plain names and pipes is matched exactly; it writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type with any leading `<plugin>:` prefix stripped (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking.", |   "description": "Compass plugin hooks. PostToolUse runs `compass context-check` after every main-agent tool call: it measures the session's context from its transcript and, the first time usage reaches 80% of the window and once more at 95%, returns `continue: false` with a stopReason suggesting /compass:handoff, so the tool that ran finishes and no next step starts; its shell skips subagent events before Python, the same prefilter the guard inverts. PostToolUse also runs `compass sync` on any vault write to regenerate index.md and the tag index, then records a capture signal for the artifact written (`handoff-written` under handoffs/, `vault-write` otherwise). Stop runs `compass capture-check`, which bumps the main-agent turn counter, evaluates whether a capture opportunity is due, and on due emits the stop-hook block contract naming the opportunity directory so the capture pass runs as a real turn rather than as prose the model can skip. SubagentStop runs `compass capture-signal` for each Compass agent under both its bare name (agents installed into a project's .claude/agents) and its plugin-scoped name (`compass:builder` and so on, as agents loaded from a plugin carry), listed side by side in the matcher because a matcher of plain names and pipes is matched exactly; it writes the finishing subagent's final report to a capture file under .compass/tmp/subagent-captures/ and records a typed signal keyed on agent_type with any leading `<plugin>:` prefix stripped (validator-finished, debug-finished, builder-finished, or subagent-finished). TeammateIdle runs the same capture-signal command; that event's payload carries only a teammate name (no agent type, no message), so it records a weak activity signal and writes no capture file. Every command first checks that the project's CLI is installed and exits 0 when it is not, so a project the plugin is loaded for but not set up in (or mid-setup) is never blocked. The guard judges subagent calls only, identified by the agent id in the event; the main agent, working with the human, is never denied, and its calls never start Python: the guard's shell reads the event and exits before the interpreter unless an agent id is present. The CLI parses each hook's stdin JSON, self-filters where relevant to avoid a write loop, and never exits 2 (which would block the user's turn). The PostToolUse entry is split three ways (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) because the `if` clause does not support `\|\|` boolean OR; each entry has its own permission-rule pattern. SessionStart additionally runs `compass self-update` (matcher startup only): refreshes the install from its recorded source - sha-gated, throttled, silent unless it updated, never blocking. The same matcher runs `compass drift --hook`: when HEAD differs from the newest row of the drift log it starts a detached scan of the vault's code references, index lines and catalog rows against git, under a lock file, and returns at once without output. SessionStart also runs `compass session-state` (matcher compact only): after a compaction it injects the session's state block (open tasks, decisions, files touched, uncommitted changes, last test run) as additional context; its shell skips subagent events before Python, matching the quoted `\"agent_id\"` or `\"agent_type\"` key so text the human typed after /compact that mentions those words still reaches the CLI. PreCompact runs the same command on every compaction, manual or auto, and prints the summary instructions that name what the summary must keep.", |  |

The index shows the frontmatter `summary` instantly and upgrades to the LLM summary moments later. The rulings inside an ADR's Decision section become discrete ID'd units, not only prose; citing the ADR document is not coverage of its individual rulings.<br>- **D-02:** The fail-loud parsing contract is required: "decisions present but unparseable" must be distinguishable from "no decisions present" and must never pass silently.<br>- **D-03:** The blocking coverage gate fires at the plan boundary: a plan is not approved while a trackable decision is unclaimed.<br>- **D-04:** Coverage extends to task level as citation and audit, not as gates: tasks cite the decision IDs they implement, and validation audits each task's cited IDs; there is no mid-build per-task blocking.<br>- **D-05:** Because statuses and phases are user-configurable ([[SPEC-009-configurable-pipeline-workflows]]), a workflow must declare which status transition carries the coverage gate; the gate is bound to a transition, not hardcoded to one phase name. | <br>## Decisions (made by the human)<br><br>- **D-01:** ADRs are in scope as decision sources. | moved | .compass/research/pipeline/RESEARCH-decision-coverage-impl.md | 37 | 397a324 | .compass/specs/SPEC-005-index-auto-maintained-and-mirrored-per-folder.md:29-37 | <br>## Decisions (made by the human)<br><br>- **The index is machine-only.** No human ever edits an `index.md`; it exists purely for AI agents to orient. So it is a fully derived artifact, regenerated from the filesystem and frontmatter on every sync - never append-only. Deletes and renames are handled for free by regeneration; there is no human content to preserve.<br>- **Lean root + per-folder.** A lean root `index.md` plus an auto-generated `index.md` in each folder listing its contents. A folder-spec keeps its authored spec body; its contents listing is a clearly-delimited, machine-regenerated section, never hand-edited.<br>- **Never stale, and agents must trust it.** Because it is a tool, run the reconcile from as many trigger points as possible (write, delete/rename, turn-end) so the index is correct at all times, and make agents aware (templates, methodology) that the index is authoritative and current - the first thing to read, never re-derived by hand.<br>- **Summaries are automatic and invisible.** A human never asks for a summary or an index update and is never aware one happened. When a page changes, a background job - spawned by the existing hook, detached so it never blocks or delays the write - generates a one-line summary with headless `claude -p`, caches it by content hash, and updates the index entry when ready. The index shows the frontmatter `summary` instantly and upgrades to the LLM summary moments later. This is the HumanLayer / Anthropic principle: a compacted summary layer for navigation, full content loaded just-in-time. (Prototype confirmed: haiku summaries are good and often beat the title; latency is 10-30s, which is why generation is detached, not in the write path.)<br>- **Bounded, capped LLM cost (must not run away).** Summaries use only a small/cheap model (haiku); run only when a page's content hash changed (never per keystroke); the summary job never re-triggers itself (its cache/index writes are excluded from the summary trigger and loop-guarded); and a hard per-window call budget governs the rest - past the budget the index keeps the frontmatter summary and defers. Worst case the feature degrades to frontmatter summaries; it can never explode token usage. Estimated steady-state cost: well under $0.005 per changed page on haiku; a 50-artifact backfill is a one-time ~$0.10-0.25.<br>- **Applied to everything on update.** Updating to this version backfills the whole vault once: it (re)builds every folder index and generates a summary for every existing artifact - specs, research, decisions, lessons, plans, all of it - with no human step. After update nothing is left un-indexed or un-summarized. | The index maintains itself on **both** add and remove, and it mirrors the folder structure: a lean root index for orientation, plus an `index.md` in each folder that auto-lists that folder's contents. Opening any folder shows what is in it; deleting a file removes it from the index without human cleanup.<br><br>## Decisions (made by the human)<br><br>- **The index is machine-only.** No human ever edits an `index.md`; it exists purely for AI agents to orient. So it is a fully derived artifact, regenerated from the filesystem and frontmatter on every sync - never append-only. Deletes and renames are handled for free by regeneration; there is no human content to preserve.<br>- **Lean root + per-folder.** A lean root `index.md` plus an auto-generated `index.md` in each folder listing its contents. A folder-spec keeps its authored spec body; its contents listing is a clearly-delimited, machine-regenerated section, never hand-edited.<br>- **Never stale, and agents must trust it.** Because it is a tool, run the reconcile from as many trigger points as possible (write, delete/rename, turn-end) so the index is correct at all times, and make agents aware (templates, methodology) that the index is authoritative and current - the first thing to read, never re-derived by hand.<br>- **Summaries are automatic and invisible.** A human never asks for a summary or an index update and is never aware one happened. When a page changes, a background job - spawned by the existing hook, detached so it never blocks or delays the write - generates a one-line summary with headless `claude -p`, caches it by content hash, and updates the index entry when ready. The index shows the frontmatter `summary` instantly and upgrades to the LLM summary moments later. This is the HumanLayer / Anthropic principle: a compacted summary layer for navigation, full content loaded just-in-time. (Prototype confirmed: haiku summaries are good and often beat the title; latency is 10-30s, which is why generation is detached, not in the write path.)<br>- **Bounded, capped LLM cost (must not run away).** Summaries use only a small/cheap model (haiku); run only when a page's content hash changed (never per keystroke); the summary job never re-triggers itself (its cache/index writes are excluded from the summary trigger and loop-guarded); and a hard per-window call budget governs the rest - past the budget the index keeps the frontmatter summary and defers. | moved | .compass/research/pipeline/RESEARCH-decision-coverage-impl.md | 149 | 397a324 | .compass/decisions/ADR-004-hierarchical-specs-with-facets.md:26-68 | <br>Adopt a four-part architecture for the Compass vault:<br><br>### Part 1: MemGPT-style three-tier memory<br><br>- **Hot tier (always in context):** root `index.md` pointers + one-line summaries + active task IDs + lessons catalog tags. Hard cap 5,000 tokens. Positioned at the START of every agent prompt.<br>- **Warm tier (fetched on navigation):** folder-level `index.md` files. Each is a RAPTOR-style summary node above its child specs - reflects what the branch is for, lists children with one-line each.<br>- **Cold tier (fetched only on demand):** full spec bodies and historical artifacts. Agent reaches these by following a pointer from hot or warm.<br><br>Movement between tiers is query-driven via explicit reads, modeled on MemGPT's page-fault analog. Eviction from hot occurs at occupancy thresholds.<br><br>### Part 2: Hierarchical folder layout<br><br>A spec earns its own folder when it has 3+ sub-concerns OR its body would exceed roughly 2,000 tokens. The folder's `index.md` is the parent spec at that level. Numbering is local per folder (resets at each level). Path is identity. Children inside follow the same rule recursively.<br><br>```<br>.compass/specs/<br>├── SPEC-001-flat-thing.md<br>├── SPEC-002-tile-editor/<br>│ ├── index.md<br>│ ├── SPEC-001-master-material.md<br>│ └── SPEC-002-brush-system/<br>│ ├── index.md<br>│ └── SPEC-001-stroke-rendering.md<br>```<br><br>### Part 3: Faceted tag overlay<br><br>Every spec carries `tags: [facet1, facet2, ...]` in frontmatter. The tags are folksonomy (free-form, not controlled vocabulary). A spec belongs to whatever facets describe it: a master material spec is `tags: [rendering, tile-editor, materials, shaders]` even though its folder is `tile-editor/`.<br><br>A generated `.compass/meta/tag-index.yaml` maps each tag to the specs that carry it. This is the multi-parent retrieval primitive. The agent reaches multi-perspective specs by reading the tag index, not by crawling folders.<br><br>### Part 4: Admission control<br><br>Before adding any new content to the hot path, Compass checks the working set: how many specs has the agent touched in the last N turns, and would adding the candidate push the hot path over its 5,000-token cap? If yes, refuse. The agent is told to use the tag index or folder navigation instead.<br><br>Working set is tracked in `.compass/meta/working-set.yaml` - rolling window of (spec_path, turns_ago) entries.<br><br>## Decision details on the four open questions<br><br>1. **Auto-generated vs hand-curated branch summaries.** Auto-generated by an LLM pass at write time, with human approval gate via `/compass:promote-spec` or `/compass:taxonomize`. Cheap and consistent; humans approve before commit.<br>2. **Tag vocabulary.** Folksonomy. Free-form `tags:` per spec. Periodic `/compass:consolidate` runs a vocabulary pass that proposes synonym merges and tag retirements - human approves.<br>3. **Lessons catalog in the hot path.** Stays as a single file (`.compass/meta/lessons-catalog.yaml`) in the hot path. Tags on specs are facets of the spec, not duplicates of lesson tags. Lessons themselves carry their own tags; the two tag namespaces are independent but can share words (no rule against it). | <br>Adopt a four-part architecture for the Compass vault:<br><br>### Part 1: MemGPT-style three-tier memory<br><br>- **Hot tier (always in context):** root `index.md` pointers + one-line summaries + active task IDs + lessons catalog tags. Hard cap 5,000 tokens. Positioned at the START of every agent prompt.<br>- **Warm tier (fetched on navigation):** folder-level `index.md` files. Each is a RAPTOR-style summary node above its child specs - reflects what the branch is for, lists children with one-line each.<br>- **Cold tier (fetched only on demand):** full spec bodies and historical artifacts. Agent reaches these by following a pointer from hot or warm.<br><br>Movement between tiers is query-driven via explicit reads, modeled on MemGPT's page-fault analog. Eviction from hot occurs at occupancy thresholds.<br><br>### Part 2: Hierarchical folder layout<br><br>A spec earns its own folder when it has 3+ sub-concerns OR its body would exceed roughly 2,000 tokens. The folder's `index.md` is the parent spec at that level. Numbering is local per folder (resets at each level). Path is identity. Children inside follow the same rule recursively.<br><br>```<br>.compass/specs/<br>├── SPEC-001-flat-thing.md<br>├── SPEC-002-tile-editor/<br>│ ├── index.md<br>│ ├── SPEC-001-master-material.md<br>│ └── SPEC-002-brush-system/<br>│ ├── index.md<br>│ └── SPEC-001-stroke-rendering.md<br>```<br><br>### Part 3: Faceted tag overlay<br><br>Every spec carries `tags: [facet1, facet2, ...]` in frontmatter. The tags are folksonomy (free-form, not controlled vocabulary). A spec belongs to whatever facets describe it: a master material spec is `tags: [rendering, tile-editor, materials, shaders]` even though its folder is `tile-editor/`.<br><br>A generated `.compass/meta/tag-index.yaml` maps each tag to the specs that carry it. This is the multi-parent retrieval primitive. The agent reaches multi-perspective specs by reading the tag index, not by crawling folders.<br><br>### Part 4: Admission control<br><br>Before adding any new content to the hot path, Compass checks the working set: how many specs has the agent touched in the last N turns, and would adding the candidate push the hot path over its 5,000-token cap? If yes, refuse. The agent is told to use the tag index or folder navigation instead.<br><br>Working set is tracked in `.compass/meta/working-set.yaml` - rolling window of (spec_path, turns_ago) entries.<br><br>## Decision details on the four open questions<br><br>1. **Auto-generated vs hand-curated branch summaries.** Auto-generated by an LLM pass at write time, with human approval gate via `/compass:promote-spec` or `/compass:taxonomize`. Cheap and consistent; humans approve before commit.<br>2. **Tag vocabulary.** Folksonomy. Free-form `tags:` per spec. Periodic `/compass:consolidate` runs a vocabulary pass that proposes synonym merges and tag retirements - human approves.<br>3. **Lessons catalog in the hot path.** Stays as a single file (`.compass/meta/lessons-catalog.yaml`) in the hot path. Tags on specs are facets of the spec, not duplicates of lesson tags. Lessons themselves carry their own tags; the two tag namespaces are independent but can share words (no rule against it). | .compass/decisions/vault-structure/ADR-004-hierarchical-specs-with-facets.md:26-68 (renamed in a49b004, similarity 100) |
